such that

\sum_{j=0}^{7}\|\widehat{\phi}\|_{C^{j}([0,L];H^{7-j}(\mathcal{D}_{-}))}\leq C\sigma(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}([0,r_{0}]))}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}([0,r_{0}])}),

where the positive constant
C
depends on
\overline{U}_{-}
,
L
and
r_{0}
.
So it follows from the embedding theorem that

\widehat{\phi}\in C^{4}(\overline{\mathcal{D}_{-}})
and

(4.47)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\phi}\|_{C^{4}(\overline{\mathcal{D}_{-}})}\leq C\sigma(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}([0,r_{0}]))}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}([0,r_{0}])}).

Moreover, the linearized problem of (
4.37
) is rotationally invariant. The same are the domain and the initial-boundary conditions. So by the uniqueness, we know the solutions of the linearized problem of (
4.37
) are actually functions of
(z,r)
, such that
\widehat{\phi}(z,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=\widehat{\varphi}(z,\sqrt{r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}+r_{3}^{2}+r_{4}^{2}})
. By the iteration argument and the uniqueness of the nonlinear problem (
4.37
)-(
4.39
), we know that the solutions of the nonlinear problem (
4.37
)-(
4.39
) are also functions of
(z,r)
.
Next, we will verify the following identities hold

(4.48)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\widehat{\varphi}(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{3}\widehat{\varphi}(z,0)=0.

By (
4.36
), we only need to prove that

(4.49)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overline{\varphi}(0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{3}\overline{\varphi}(0)=0,

(4.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\varphi(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{3}\varphi(z,0)=0.

By
\partial_{r}^{j}(\bar{\rho}_{-},\bar{q}_{-})(0)=0
,
j=1,3
, it is easy to see that (
4.49
) holds.

Next, it follows from (
4.20
) and
\psi=r^{2}\varphi
that

(4.51)

\displaystyle r\rho u=\psi_{r}=2r\varphi+r^{2}\varphi_{r}.

Then

(4.52)

\displaystyle 3\varphi_{r}+r\varphi_{rr}=\partial_{r}(\rho u)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad 5\partial_{r}^{3}\varphi+r\partial_{r}^{4}\varphi=\partial_{r}^{3}(\rho u).

They yield that

(4.53)

\displaystyle 3\partial_{r}\varphi(z,0)=\partial_{r}(\rho u)(z,0)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad 5\partial_{r}^{3}\varphi(z,0)=\partial_{r}^{3}(\rho u)(z,0).

So in order to show (
4.48
),
it suffices to prove that

(4.54)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(\rho u)(z,0)=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\partial_{r}^{3}(\rho u)(z,0)=0.

Taking derivative with respect to the variable
r
on the both sides of (
4.19
) and applying
v(z,0)=0
and
\partial_{r}s(0,0)=0
, we have

(4.55)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}s(z,0)=0.

Similarly, by
v(z,0)=0
and
\partial_{r}(p,q,s)(0,0)=0
, (
4.18
) yields that

(4.56)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}B(z,0)=0.

(
2.3
) and (
2.5
) yield that

(4.57)

\displaystyle\rho uv_{z}+\rho vv_{r}+p_{r}-\frac{1}{r}\rho w^{2}=0.

By
v(z,0)=0
and
w(z,0)=0
, (
4.57
) yields that

(4.58)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}p(z,0)=0.

(
4.55
), (
4.56
) and (
4.58
) imply that

(4.59)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\rho(z,0)=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\partial_{r}q(z,0)=0.

Taking derivative with respect to the variable
r
on the both sides of (
4.15
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}u\partial_{z}\rho+u\partial_{z}(\partial_{r}\rho)+v\partial_{r}^{2}\rho+\partial_{r}v\partial_{r}\rho+\partial_{r}\rho(\partial_{z}u+\partial_{r}u)

(4.60)

\displaystyle+\rho(\partial_{z}(\partial_{r}u)+\partial_{r}^{2}v)+\partial_{r}\rho\frac{v}{r}+\rho\partial_{r}(\frac{v}{r})=0.

By (
4.59
) and
v(z,0)=0
, then

(4.61)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}v(z,0)=0.

Taking twice derivatives with respect to the variable
r
on the both sides of (
4.17
) and applying
\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{w}(0)=0
,
\partial_{r}^{2}w_{en}(0,0)=0
,
w(z,0)=0
and (
4.61
), we have

(4.62)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}w(z,0)=0.

Taking twice derivative with respect to the variable
r
on the both sides of (
4.57
) and employing (
4.59
), (
4.61
), (
4.62
),
v(z,0)=0
,
w(z,0)=0
, we have

(4.63)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{3}p(z,0)=0.

Then

(4.64)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{3}\rho(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{3}q(z,0)=0.

By (
4.59
), (
4.61
), (
4.64
) and
v(z,0)=0
, we obtain (
4.54
). Therefore, (
4.48
) is verified, and then it follows from Lemma
4.1
that

(4.65)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\varphi}\|_{C^{4}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C\sigma(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}([0,r_{0}]))}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}([0,r_{0}])}).

Hence
U_{-}\in C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})
and (
2.45
) holds.
∎

5.
Linearized problem in the quasi-subsonic domain

In this section, we will reformulate the nonlinear free boundary value problem in the quasi-subsonic domain behind the shock front into a fixed nonlinear boundary value problem. Then we will linearize the problem and establish the well-posedness theorems of it by introducing two auxiliary problems for the two elliptic equations and then solving the remaining three transport equations. The well-posedness theorems of the linearized problem play an important role in solving the nonlinear free boundary value problem in the next section.

5.1.
Reformulation of the nonlinear free boundary value problem in the quasi-subsonic domain behind the shock front

By characteristic decomposition, the equations (
2.3
)-(
2.7
) can be rewritten as follows:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\rho q^{2}}\partial_{z}p-\frac{M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{\rho q^{2}}\partial_{r}p-\partial_{r}\theta-\frac{\cos\theta\sin\theta}{r}=0,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\rho q^{2}\partial_{z}\theta-\frac{\rho q^{2}M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\partial_{r}\theta+\frac{1-M^{2}}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\partial_{r}p-\frac{\rho q^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{r(1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta)}-\frac{\rho w^{2}}{r}=0,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(rw)+\tan\theta\cdot\partial_{r}(rw)=0,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}{B}+\tan\theta\cdot\partial_{r}{B}=0,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}s+\tan\theta\cdot\partial_{r}s=0,

where the Bernoulli’s function

{B}:=\frac{1}{2}(q^{2}+w^{2})+\frac{\gamma p}{(\gamma-1)\rho}
.
Next, the R.-H. conditions (
2.10
)-(
2.14
) can be rewritten as

(5.6)

\displaystyle G_{1}(U_{+},U_{-}):=

\displaystyle[\rho u][p+\rho v^{2}]-[\rho uv][\rho v]=0,

(5.7)

\displaystyle G_{2}(U_{+},U_{-}):=

\displaystyle[p+\rho u^{2}][p+\rho v^{2}]-([\rho uv])^{2}=0,

(5.8)

\displaystyle G_{3}(U_{+},U_{-}):=

\displaystyle[w]=0,

(5.9)

\displaystyle G_{4}(U_{+},U_{-}):=

\displaystyle[\frac{1}{2}q^{2}+i]=0,

(5.10)

\displaystyle G_{5}(U_{+},U_{-};\varphi^{\prime}):=

\displaystyle[\rho uv]-\varphi^{\prime}[p+\rho v^{2}]=0.

In order to fix and flatten the shock front
\Gamma_{s}
, which is a free boundary, let us introduce the coordinates transformation

(5.11)

\displaystyle\pounds_{K}:(\tilde{z},\tilde{r})=\Big(L+\displaystyle\frac{L-K}{L-{\varphi}(r)}(z-L),\,r\Big),

where constant
K\in(0,L)
is the approximating shock location, which is uniquely determined and will be given in the next section. We expect
|\varphi(r)-K|
to be small, so
\pounds_{K}
is invertible and its inverse is

(5.12)

\displaystyle\pounds_{K}^{-1}:(z,\,r)=\big(L+\frac{L-\varphi(\tilde{r})}{L-K}(\tilde{z}-L),\,\tilde{r}\big).

Then the domain
\Omega_{+}
becomes

\displaystyle\Omega_{+}^{K}:=\{(z,r)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:K<z<L,0<r<r_{0}\}.

For the notational simplicity, we drop "
\widetilde{}
" in the sequel argument.

By tedious calculations, we can linearize equations (
5.1
)-(
5.5
) for
U_{+}=\overline{U}_{+}+\delta U
and
\varphi^{\prime}=\delta\varphi^{\prime}
as follows.

(5.13)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\Big)-\partial_{r}\Big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}\Big)=r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}{f}_{1}(\delta U,\varphi),

(5.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\Big)-\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\delta{w}+\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r\gamma c_{v}}\delta{s}=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}{f}_{2}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\varphi),

(5.15)

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)(r\delta w)+\partial_{r}(r\bar{w}_{+})\cdot\delta\theta={f}_{3}(\delta U,\varphi),

(5.16)

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)\delta B+\partial_{r}\bar{B}_{+}\cdot\delta\theta=f_{4}(\delta U,\varphi),

(5.17)

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)\delta s+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}\cdot\delta\theta={f}_{5}(\delta U,\varphi),

where

(5.18)

\displaystyle H(z,r):=

\displaystyle\frac{(L-\varphi)\tan\delta\theta}{L-K+(z-L)\delta\varphi^{\prime}\tan\delta\theta},

\displaystyle{f}_{1}(\delta U,\varphi):=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}-\frac{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\rho q^{2}}\Big)\partial_{z}\delta{p}+\frac{M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{\rho q^{2}}\partial_{r}\delta p

\displaystyle+\frac{\cos\theta\sin\theta-\delta\theta}{r}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}^{2}}{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{\rho q^{2}}-\frac{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\delta{\theta}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{(z-L)\delta\varphi^{\prime}}{L-\varphi}\Big(\frac{M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{\rho q^{2}}\partial_{z}\delta p+\partial_{z}\delta\theta\Big)

(5.19)

\displaystyle-\frac{\varphi-K}{L-\varphi}\frac{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}{\rho q^{2}}\partial_{z}\delta p,

\displaystyle{f}_{2}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\varphi):=

\displaystyle-\big(\rho q^{2}-\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\big)\partial_{z}\delta\theta+\frac{M^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\Big(\partial_{r}\delta p+\frac{1}{r}\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\rho q^{2}M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\partial_{r}\delta\theta+\frac{1}{r}\frac{\rho q^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{r}\Big(\rho w^{2}-\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}-\frac{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\delta{p}-2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}\delta{w}+\frac{1}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}\delta{s}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{(z-L)\delta\varphi^{\prime}}{L-\varphi}\Big(\frac{\rho q^{2}M^{2}\cos\theta\sin\theta}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\partial_{z}\delta\theta-\frac{1-M^{2}}{1-M^{2}\cos^{2}\theta}\partial_{z}\delta p\Big)

(5.20)

\displaystyle-\frac{\varphi-K}{L-\varphi}\rho q^{2}\partial_{z}\delta\theta,

(5.21)

\displaystyle{f}_{3}(\delta U,\varphi):=

\displaystyle-\partial_{r}(r\bar{w}_{+})\cdot\big(H(z,r)-\delta\theta\big),

(5.22)

\displaystyle f_{4}(\delta U,\varphi):=

\displaystyle-\partial_{r}\bar{B}_{+}\cdot\big(H(z,r)-\delta\theta\big),

(5.23)

\displaystyle{f}_{5}(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime},\delta z_{*}):=

\displaystyle-\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}\cdot\big(H(z,r)-\delta\theta\big).

The boundary conditions in the new coordinates are

(i).

On the wall of the nozzle
\Gamma_{w}\cap\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}}
,

(5.24)

\displaystyle\delta\theta=0;

(ii).

On the symmetry axis
\Gamma_{a}\cap\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}}
,

(5.25)

\displaystyle\delta\theta=0,\quad\delta w=0;

(iii).

On the exit of the nozzle
\Gamma_{ex}
,

(5.26)

\delta p=\sigma p_{ex}(r),\quad\text{on}\quad\Gamma_{ex};

(iv).

On the shock front
\Gamma_{K}:=\{z=K,r\in(0,r_{0})\}
,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\alpha_{j}^{+}\cdot\delta U=G_{j}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\delta U_{-},\varphi),\quad{j=1,2,3,4},

(5.28)

\displaystyle\alpha_{5}^{+}\cdot\delta U-[\bar{p}]\delta{\varphi}^{\prime}=G_{5}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\delta U_{-},\varphi),

where
{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{j}^{+}={{\nabla_{{U}_{+}}}}G_{j}(\overline{U}_{+},\overline{U}_{-})
,
{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{5}^{+}={{\nabla_{{U}_{+}}}}G_{5}(\overline{U}_{+},\overline{U}_{-};0)
, and

(5.29)

\displaystyle G_{j}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\delta U_{-},\varphi):=\alpha_{j}^{+}\cdot\delta U-G_{j}(U,U_{-}(\varphi^{\prime},\varphi(r_{0}))),

(5.30)

\displaystyle G_{5}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\delta U_{-},\varphi):=\alpha_{5}^{+}\cdot\delta U-[\bar{p}]\delta\varphi^{\prime}-G_{5}(U,U_{-}(\varphi^{\prime},\varphi(r_{0}));\varphi^{\prime}).

By straightforward computation, we have

(5.31)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1}^{+}=\Big(\frac{\bar{q}_{+}}{\bar{c}_{+}^{2}},\,0,\,0,\,\bar{\rho}_{+},\,-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\Big)^{\top},

(5.32)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2}^{+}=\Big(1+\overline{M}_{+}^{2},\,0,\,0,\,2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+},\,-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{\gamma c_{v}}\Big)^{\top},\quad{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{3}^{+}=\Big(0,\,0,\,1,\,0,\,0\Big)^{\top},

(5.33)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{4}^{+}=\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}},\,0,\,0,\,\bar{q}_{+},\,\frac{\bar{p}_{+}}{(\gamma-1)c_{v}\bar{\rho}_{+}}\Big)^{\top},\quad{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{5}^{+}=\Big(0,\,\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2},\,0,\,0,\,0\Big)^{\top}.

Thus, the boundary conditions (
5.27
) can be rewritten as

A_{s}(\delta{p},\delta{w},\delta{q},\delta{s})^{\top}=\mathbf{G}:=(G_{1}^{\sharp},G_{2}^{\sharp},G_{3}^{\sharp},G_{4}^{\sharp})^{\top},

where

A_{s}:=\begin{pmatrix}\displaystyle\frac{\bar{q}_{+}}{\bar{c}_{+}^{2}}&0&\bar{\rho}_{+}&-\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\\
1+\overline{M}_{+}^{2}&0&2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}&-\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{\gamma c_{v}}\\
0&1&0&0\\
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}}&0&\bar{q}_{+}&\displaystyle\frac{\bar{p}_{+}}{(\gamma-1)c_{v}\bar{\rho}_{+}}\end{pmatrix}.

It is easy to see that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\det A_{s}=\displaystyle\frac{\bar{p}_{+}}{(\gamma-1)c_{v}}(1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2})\neq 0,\quad\text{as}\quad\overline{M}_{+}\neq 1,

so we have the following boundary conditions on
\Gamma_{s}

(5.35)

\big(\delta{p},\,\delta{w},\,\delta{q},\,\delta{s}\big)=\big(g_{1},\,g_{2},\,g_{3},\,g_{4}\big):=A_{s}^{-1}\mathbf{G}.

Moreover, it follows from (
5.28
) that

(5.36)

\displaystyle\delta{\varphi}^{\prime}=g_{5}:=\left(\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{5}^{+}\cdot\delta U-G_{5}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\delta U_{-},\varphi)}{[\bar{p}]}\right).

Finally, because the elliptic equation (
5.14
) is coupled with the hyperbolic equations (
5.15
) and (
5.17
), we need to further reformulate the elliptic equation (
5.14
).
Consider the Cauchy problems of equations (
5.15
) and (
5.17
):

(5.37)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)(r\delta w)+\partial_{r}(r\bar{w}_{+})\cdot\delta\theta={f}_{3}(\delta U,\varphi)&\text{in}\quad{\Omega}_{+}^{K},\\[3.0pt]
\delta w(K,r)=g_{2}(K,r);\end{cases}

(5.38)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)\delta s+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}\delta\theta={f}_{5}(\delta U,\varphi)&\text{in}\quad{\Omega}_{+}^{K},\\[3.0pt]
\delta s(K,r)=g_{4}(K,r).\end{cases}

For any fixed point
(z,r)
, let
\tau\mapsto R(\tau;z,{r})
be the characteristic curve passing through
(z,{r})
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}R(\tau;z,{r})}{\mathrm{d}\tau}=

\displaystyle H(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r})),\quad\tau\in[K,z],

\displaystyle R(z;z,{r})=

\displaystyle{r}.

Then

(5.39)

\begin{array}[]{rl}&r\delta w(z,r)\\[5.69054pt]
=&R(K;z,r)g_{2}(R(K;z,r))+\int_{K}^{z}{f}_{3}(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau\\[5.69054pt]
&-\int_{K}^{z}\Big(R(\tau;z,{r})\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))+\bar{w}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))\Big)\delta\theta(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau\end{array}

and

(5.40)

\begin{array}[]{rl}\delta s(z,r)=&g_{4}(R(K;z,r))+\int_{K}^{z}{f}_{5}(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau\\[5.69054pt]
&-\int_{K}^{z}\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))\delta\theta(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau.\end{array}

Substituting (
5.39
) and (
5.40
) into (
5.14
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\Big)

(5.41)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}(\delta U,\delta\varphi),

where

\displaystyle f_{2}(\delta U,\varphi)

\displaystyle:={f}_{2}^{\sharp}(\delta U,\varphi)+\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r^{2}}\Big\{R(K;z,r)g_{2}(R(K;z,r))+\int_{K}^{z}f_{3}(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle-\int_{K}^{z}\Big(R(\tau;z,{r})\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))+\bar{w}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))\Big)\delta\theta(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+\Big(r\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}(r)+\bar{w}_{+}(r)\Big)\int_{K}^{z}\delta\theta(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau\Big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\gamma c_{v}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}}{r}\Big\{g_{4}(R(K;z,r))+\int_{K}^{z}f_{5}(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau

(5.42)

\displaystyle-\int_{K}^{z}\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))\delta\theta(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}(r)\int_{K}^{z}\delta\theta(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau\Big\}.

Remark 5.1
.

There is a nonlocal term
\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau
in (
5.41
). This is quite different from
[
14
,
20
,
26
,
36
,
37
,
35
,
38
]
and shows the spectacular influence of non-zero swirl. It introduces stronger coupling in the Euler equations with integral-type nonlocal terms.

5.2.
Linearized boundary value problems for the two elliptic equations

In this and the next subsections, we will consider the linearised boundary value problem by assuming that
f_{i}
for
i=1,\cdots,5
and the values of
(p,w,q,s)
on the shock front are given in some suitable Hölder norm spaces. Then we will establish the unique existence of the solutions to the linearized equations (
5.13
), (
5.15
)-(
5.17
) and (
5.41
), with boundary conditions (
5.24
)-(
5.30
).

First, let us consider the boundary value problem for the two elliptic equations (
5.13
) and (
5.41
) in
\Omega_{+}^{K}

(5.43)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\Big)-\partial_{r}\Big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}\Big)=r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(z,r),

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\Big)

(5.44)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}(z,r),

where the boundary conditions are

(5.45)

\displaystyle\delta{\theta}=0\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{on }\Gamma_{a}\cup\Gamma_{w}

(5.46)

\displaystyle\delta{p}=\sigma p_{ex}(r)\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{on }\Gamma_{ex}

(5.47)

\displaystyle\delta{p}=g_{1}\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{on }\Gamma_{K}.

For the given functions
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,
g_{1}
and
p_{ex}
, we assume that

(5.48)

\displaystyle f_{1}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}}),\quad\partial_{r}f_{1}(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}f_{1}(z,r_{0})=0,

(5.49)

\displaystyle f_{2}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}}),\quad f_{2}(z,0)=0,\quad f_{2}(z,r_{0})=0,

(5.50)

\displaystyle g_{1}\in C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}]),\quad p_{ex}\in C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}]),

(5.51)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}g_{1}(K,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}g_{1}(K,r_{0})=0,\quad\partial_{r}p_{ex}(L,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}p_{ex}(L,r_{0})=0.

Remark 5.2
.

the regularity assumed for
f_{i}
,
i=1,2
in (
5.48
) is different from the one for
f_{i}
,
i=3,4,5
in (
5.173
) due to the symmetry axis singularity of the swirl velocity
w
.

In this section, we will show the following theorem on the unique existence of solutions for the bondary value problem (
5.43
)-(
5.47
).

Theorem 5.3
.

Suppose that conditions (
5.48
)-(
5.51
) hold. There exists a positive constant
r_{*}
, only depending on
\overline{U}_{-}(r)
and
\gamma
, such that for
0<r_{0}\leq r_{*}
, there exists a unique solution
(\delta p,\delta\theta)
for the boundary value problem (
5.43
)-(
5.47
), if and only if

(5.52)

\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\sigma p_{ex}(r)-{g}_{1}(K,r)\Big)\mathrm{d}r=\int\int_{\Omega_{+}^{K}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(z,r)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r.

Moreover, the solution
(\delta p,\delta\theta)
satisfies the following estimate:

\displaystyle\|\delta p\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}

(5.53)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
K
,
L
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
and
\alpha
.

To prove Theorem
5.3
, we need to handle the inhomogeneous terms in the linearised equations (
5.43
)-(
5.44
). To make it, we introduce two auxilliary problems by letting
(\delta p,\delta\theta)=(\delta p_{1}+\delta p_{2},\delta\theta_{1}+\delta\theta_{2})
, where
(\delta p_{1},\delta\theta_{1})
and
(\delta p_{2},\delta\theta_{2})
satisfy the following two linear boundary value problems, respectively.

\llbracket\textit{Problem I}\rrbracket
:
(\delta p_{1},\delta\theta_{1})
satisfies the following linear boundary value problem in
\Omega_{+}^{K}

(5.54)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}_{1}\Big)-\partial_{r}\Big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}_{1}\Big)=r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(z,r),

(5.55)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}_{1}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}_{1}\Big)=0,

where the boundary conditions are

(5.56)

\displaystyle\delta{\theta}_{1}=0\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{a}\cup\Gamma_{w}

(5.57)

\displaystyle\delta{p}_{1}=\sigma p_{ex}\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{ex}

(5.58)

\displaystyle\delta{p}_{1}=g_{1}\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{K}.

\llbracket\textit{Problem II}\rrbracket
:
(\delta p_{2},\delta\theta_{2})
satisfies the following linear boundary value problem in
\Omega_{+}^{K}

(5.59)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}_{2}\Big)-\partial_{r}\Big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}_{2}\Big)=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}_{2}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}_{2}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau

(5.60)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}(z,r)+\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}_{1}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau,

where the boundary conditions are

(5.61)

\displaystyle\delta{\theta}_{2}=0\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{a}\cup\Gamma_{w}

(5.62)

\displaystyle\delta{p}_{2}=0\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{ex}

(5.63)

\displaystyle\delta{p}_{2}=0\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{K}.

Then Theorem
5.3
will be proven by establishing the unique existence of solutions of
\llbracket\textit{Problem I}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket\textit{Problem II}\rrbracket
. It is Lemma
5.4
, Lemma
5.5
and Lemma
5.6
.

5.2.1.
Well-posedness of
\llbracket\textit{Problem I}\rrbracket

By (
5.55
), there exists a potential function
\Phi
such that

(5.64)

\displaystyle\nabla\Phi=(\partial_{z}\Phi,\partial_{r}\Phi)=\Big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}_{1},-\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}_{1}\Big).

Substituting (
5.64
) into (
5.54
), we have

(5.65)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\Phi\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{r}\Phi\Big)+\frac{1}{r}\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{r}\Phi=\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1},

where the boundary conditions become

(5.66)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\Phi=0\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{on }\Gamma_{a}\cup\Gamma_{w}

(5.67)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Phi=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}g_{1}(K,r)\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{on }\Gamma_{K}

(5.68)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Phi=\sigma\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}p_{ex}(r)\qquad

\displaystyle\mbox{on }\Gamma_{ex}.

Obviously, to study
\llbracket\textit{Problem I}\rrbracket
, it is sufficient to study boundary value problem (
5.65
)-(
5.68
). To deal with the singularity at the symmetry axis
r=0
, let us consider the problem in three dimensions. That is, introduce

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=(z,r\cos\tau,r\sin\tau).

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}:=

\displaystyle\big\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:K<x_{1}<L,\,0\leq x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}<r_{0}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{K}:=

\displaystyle\big\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:x_{1}=K,\,0\leq x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}<r_{0}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{ex}:=

\displaystyle\big\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:x_{1}=L,\,0\leq x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}<r_{0}\big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{w}:=

\displaystyle\big\{(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:K<x_{1}<L,\,x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}=r_{0}\big\}.

Let
\phi(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=\Phi(x_{1},\sqrt{x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}})
.
Then problem (
5.65
)-(
5.68
) can be rewritten as the following
\llbracket\textit{Problem I(3D)}\rrbracket
:

(5.69)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\phi\Big)+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\Big)=\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(x_{1},r),\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{P},

with the boundary conditions:

(5.70)

\displaystyle x_{2}\partial_{x_{2}}\phi(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})+x_{3}\partial_{x_{3}}\phi(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=0,

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{w},

(5.71)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\phi(K,x_{2},x_{3})=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}g_{1}(K,r),

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{K},

(5.72)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\phi(L,x_{2},x_{3})=\sigma\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}p_{ex}(r),

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{ex}.

It follows from assumptions (
5.48
)-(
5.51
) and Lemmas
3.1
and
4.1
that the coefficients in equation (
5.69
) are
C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})
and

\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{K}})}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{ex}})}\leq C(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}).

For the boundary value problem (
5.69
)-(
5.72
), we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 5.4
.

There exists a unique weak solution
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})
up to a constant for the
\llbracket\textit{Problem I(3D)}\rrbracket
if and only if

(5.73)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\sigma p_{ex}(r)-{g}_{1}(K,r)\Big)\mathrm{d}r=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\int_{K}^{L}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(z,r)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r.

Select the solution
\phi
with the additional condition that
\int_{\mathcal{P}}\phi\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=0
.
Then if the conditions (
5.50
), (
5.48
) and (
5.51
) hold, any weak solution
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})
of the
\llbracket\textit{Problem I(3D)}\rrbracket
has a higher regularity with
\phi\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})
and satisfies the following estimates

(5.74)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\leq C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{K}})}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{ex}})}\Big),

where the constant
C
depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
,
L
and
\alpha
. Moreover,

(5.75)

\phi(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\equiv\Phi(x_{1},\sqrt{x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}}),

where
\Phi\in C^{2,\alpha}(\Omega_{+}^{K})
is a solution of
\llbracket\textit{Problem I}\rrbracket
, and it holds that

\displaystyle\|\delta\theta_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta p_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}

(5.76)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
K
,
L
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
and
\alpha
.

Proof.

This proof is divided into two steps.

Step 1.
In this step, we will show the existence of weak solution
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})
.
Let

\displaystyle G(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\frac{g_{1}(K,r)-\sigma p_{ex}(r)}{2(K-L)}(x_{1}-K)^{2}+g_{1}(K,r)x_{1}\Big).

Let

\phi_{*}:=\phi-G.

Then it follows from (
5.51
) that
\phi_{*}
satisfies the following boundary value problem

(5.77)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\phi_{*}\Big)+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi_{*}\Big)=F_{*},\quad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{P},

where

\displaystyle F_{*}:=\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(x_{1},r)-\partial_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}G\Big)-\sum_{i=2}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}G\Big),

with boundary conditions

(5.78)

\displaystyle x_{2}\partial_{x_{2}}\phi_{*}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})+x_{3}\partial_{x_{3}}\phi_{*}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=0,

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{w},

(5.79)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\phi_{*}(K,x_{2},x_{3})=0,

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{K},

(5.80)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\phi_{*}(L,x_{2},x_{3})=0,

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{ex}.

\phi_{*}
is a weak solution of the problem (
5.77
)-(
5.80
) if and only if for any test function
\zeta\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})
,

(5.81)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\phi\partial_{x_{1}}\zeta+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta\Big)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=\int_{\mathcal{P}}F_{*}\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}.

By choosing
\zeta=1
in (
5.81
), we have that
\int_{\mathcal{P}}F_{*}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=0
, that is

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}F_{1}(x_{1},r)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}}\Big(\partial_{x_{1}}\big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}G\big)+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}G\big)\Big)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

(5.82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}_{ex}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}G\mathrm{d}S-\int_{\mathcal{P}_{K}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}G\mathrm{d}S.

It is condition (
5.73
).

Next, we will prove that if (
5.82
) holds (equivalently (
5.73
) holds), there exists a weak solution
\phi_{*}
to the problem (
5.77
)-(
5.80
). Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}:=\Big\{\phi_{*}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})\Big|\int_{\mathcal{P}}\phi_{*}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=0\Big\}.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[\phi,\zeta]:=-\int_{\mathcal{P}}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\phi\partial_{x_{1}}\zeta+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}\phi\partial_{x_{i}}\zeta\Big)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}.

It is easy to see that for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}
and
\zeta\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}[\phi,\zeta]|\leq 3\Big\|\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}([0,r_{0}])}\int_{\mathcal{P}}|D\phi||D\zeta|\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\|\phi\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{P})}\|\zeta\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{P})},

where the positive constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{+}
.

On the other hand, by the Poincaré inequality, we have for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P})}^{2}=\int_{\mathcal{P}}|\phi-\int_{\mathcal{P}}\phi\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\leq C\int_{\mathcal{P}}|D\phi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}.

Thus for any
\phi\in\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{B}[\phi,\phi]|\geq C\|\phi\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{P})}^{2}.

By applying Lax-Milgram theorem, for all
F_{*}\in L^{2}
with
\int_{\mathcal{P}}F_{*}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=0
,
i.e.
, (
5.82
) holds, there exists a unique
\phi_{*}\in\mathcal{S}
such that for any
\zeta\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})

\displaystyle(F_{*},\zeta)_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P})}=

\displaystyle(F_{*},\int_{\mathcal{P}}\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x})_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P})}+(F_{*},\zeta-\int_{\mathcal{P}}\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x})_{L^{2}(\mathcal{P})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle B[\phi_{*},\zeta-\int_{\mathcal{P}}\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}]=B[\phi_{*},\zeta].

Therefore,
\phi_{*}\in\mathcal{S}\subset H^{1}(\mathcal{P})
is a weak solution. Notice that
(\phi_{*}+\text{constant.})
is also a weak solution and the solution is unique in
\mathcal{S}
. So solution
\phi_{*}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{P})
is unique up to a constant.
Moreover, choose the solution
\phi
with the additional condition that
\int_{\mathcal{P}}\phi\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=0
, then the following estimate holds

(5.83)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{H^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\leq C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{K}})}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{ex}})}\Big),

where the constant
{C}
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
and
\alpha
.

Step 2.
In this step, we will prove (
5.74
)-(
5.76
). Notice that we can multiply corresponding coefficient functions in (
5.77
) to make the boundary value problem (
5.77
)-(
5.80
) conormal, such that we can apply the results in Chapter 5 in
[
29
]
. First, by
[
29
, Theorem 5.36 and Theorem 5.45]
with
u
and
-u
to have

(5.84)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\leq C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{K}})}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{ex}})}+\|\phi\|_{{H^{1}}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\Big).

Then we can apply
[
29
, Theorem 5.36 and Theorem 5.45]
for
\phi
locally near the boundary and apply the standard De Giorgi type interior Hölder estimates (see
[
22
, Theorem 8.24]
) to have the local Hölder estimates

(5.85)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\leq C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{K}})}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{ex}})}+\|\phi\|_{{H^{1}}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\Big).

Now we can apply
[
29
, Theorem 4.6]
to have

(5.86)

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\leq C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{K}})}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{P}_{ex}})}+\|\phi\|_{{H^{1}}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\Big).

Next, we will improve the regularity of the solution
\phi
to establish (
5.74
). Because the interior estimates are standard, we only estimate
\phi
when point
\mathbf{x}
is near the points
(K,r_{0})
or
(L,r_{0})
. Without loss of the generality and for the shortness, we only consider the point near
(K,r_{0})
, since the other cases can be treated similarly.

By boundary condition
\partial_{r}\Phi(x_{1},r_{0})=0
on the nozzle wall, we can extend the solution across the nozzle wall evenly by defining

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}(x_{1},r):=\begin{cases}\Phi(x_{1},r),\quad 0<r<r_{0},\\
\Phi(x_{1},2r_{0}-r),\quad r\geq r_{0}.\end{cases}

Let

\widetilde{\phi}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=\widetilde{\Phi}(x_{1},\sqrt{x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}})=\widetilde{\Phi}(x_{1},r).

Similarly, by assumptions
\partial_{r}f_{1}(z,r_{0})=0
and
\partial_{r}g_{1}(z,r_{0})=0
, we also define

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{1}(x_{1},r):=\begin{cases}f_{1}(x_{1},r),\quad 0<r<r_{0},\\
f_{1}(x_{1},2r_{0}-r),\quad r\geq r_{0}.\end{cases}\widetilde{g}_{1}(K,r):=\begin{cases}g_{1}(K,r),\quad 0<r<r_{0},\\
g_{1}(K,2r_{0}-r),\quad r\geq r_{0}.\end{cases}

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{P}
be a small neighbourhood near point
(K,r_{0})
. Let the extended domain be

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}:=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{P}\big)\cup\big\{(z,2r_{0}-r):(z,r)\in\big(\mathcal{P}_{\tau}\cap\mathcal{P}\big)\big\}.

Then it follows from Lemma
3.1
that the extended functions satisfy the following boundary value problem

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1-\bar{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{\phi}\Big)+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}\widetilde{\phi}\Big)=\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\widetilde{f_{1}}\qquad

\displaystyle\text{in}\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}},

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\widetilde{\phi}(K,x_{2},x_{3})=\widetilde{g}_{1}(K,r),\qquad

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad x_{1}=K.

Then for any smooth domain
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}^{\sharp}\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}
, we easily have

(5.87)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}^{\sharp})}\leq C\Big(\|\widetilde{f_{1}}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}})}+\|\widetilde{g}_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(x_{1}=K)}+\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}\backslash\{x_{1}=K\})}\Big).

So it follows from (
5.86
) and (
5.87
) that

(5.88)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\mathcal{P}_{\tau}^{\sharp})}\leq\|\widetilde{\phi}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}^{\sharp})}\leq C\Big(\|\widetilde{f_{1}}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}})}+\|\widetilde{g}_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(x_{1}=K)}+\|\phi\|_{{H^{1}}(\overline{\mathcal{P}})}\Big),

where
\mathcal{P}_{\tau}^{\sharp}=\widetilde{\mathcal{P}_{\tau}}^{\sharp}\cap\overline{\mathcal{P}}
.
Now by applying the standard identity decomposition technique to combining the local estimates, similar to(
5.88
), near all the four corner points
(K,0)
,
(K,r_{0})
,
(L,0)
and
(L,r_{0})
, we can obtain (
5.74
) from (
5.83
).

By the uniqueness of the solutions of problem (
5.69
)-(
5.72
) by selecting the solutions to satisfy
\int_{\mathcal{P}}\phi\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}=0
, it follows from the fact that (
5.69
)-(
5.72
) is rotational invariant, that is, (
5.75
) holds. It follows from Lemma
4.1
and the regularity of
\phi
that boundary conditions (
5.66
) hold. Moreover, (
5.74
) yields that

(5.89)

\displaystyle\|\Phi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big).

Finally, (
5.76
) follows from (
5.64
) and (
5.89
).

∎

5.2.2.
Well-posedness of
\llbracket\textit{Problem II}\rrbracket

Now let us consider
\llbracket\textit{Problem II}\rrbracket
.
By (
5.59
), there exists a potential function
\widehat{\Psi}
such that

\displaystyle\nabla\widehat{\Psi}=(\partial_{z}\widehat{\Psi},\partial_{r}\widehat{\Psi})=

\displaystyle\Big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}_{2},\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}_{2}\Big),

which implies that

(5.90)

\displaystyle\delta{\theta}_{2}=\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\partial_{z}\widehat{\Psi},\quad\delta{p}_{2}=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\partial_{r}\widehat{\Psi}.

Then boundary conditions (
5.61
)-(
5.63
) become the tangential derivative of
\widehat{\Psi}
along all the boundaries is zero. So
\widehat{\Psi}
is a constant along the boundary.
Note that
\widehat{\Psi}
is unique up to a constant, so by substracting a possible constant, the boundary conditions (
5.61
)-(
5.63
) become

(5.91)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}=0\qquad\text{on}\quad\partial\Omega_{+}^{K}.

Let
\Psi=\frac{\widehat{\Psi}}{r^{2}}
, then

(5.92)

\displaystyle\Psi=0\qquad\text{on}\quad\partial\Omega_{+}^{K}\backslash\Gamma_{a}.

On
\Gamma_{a}
, it follows from Lemma
3.1
, equation (
5.60
) and boundary conditions (
5.56
) and (
5.61
) that
\partial_{r}(\delta p_{2})(z,0)=0
. Note that

\Psi(z,0)=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{\widehat{\Psi}}{r^{2}}=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{\widehat{\Psi}_{r}}{2r}=\frac{(1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2})\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}(0)\delta p_{2}(z,0).

Then

\displaystyle\Psi_{r}(z,0)=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{\Psi(z,r)-\Psi(z,0)}{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{\widehat{\Psi}(z,r)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}(0)}{\bar{\rho}_{+}(0)\bar{q}_{+}^{2}(0)}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(0)\delta{p}_{2}(z,0)r^{2}}{r^{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{\widehat{\Psi}_{r}(z,r)-\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}(0)}{\bar{\rho}_{+}(0)\bar{q}_{+}^{2}(0)}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(0)\delta{p}_{2}(z,0)r}{3r^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}(r)}{\bar{\rho}_{+}(r)\bar{q}_{+}^{2}(r)}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(r)\delta{p}_{2}(z,r)-\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}(0)}{\bar{\rho}_{+}(0)\bar{q}_{+}^{2}(0)}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(0)\delta{p}_{2}(z,0)}{3r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

So we have the boundary condition

(5.93)

\Psi_{r}=0\qquad\mbox{on }\Gamma_{a}.

Moreover, it follows from (
5.90
) that

(5.94)

\displaystyle\delta{\theta}_{2}=r\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\Psi,\quad\delta{p}_{2}=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(r\partial_{r}\Psi+2\Psi\Big).

By plugging (
5.94
) into equation (
5.60
), we know
\Psi
satisfies the following equation

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}{\Psi}\Big)+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\big(r\partial_{r}{\Psi}+2{\Psi}\big)\Big)

(5.95)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}{\Psi}={F}^{\sharp}(z,r).

where

{F}^{\sharp}(z,r):=\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}{F}_{2}(z,r)}{r}

and

(5.96)

{F}_{2}(z,r):=f_{2}(z,r)+\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}_{1}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau.

By (
2.31
) and (
2.39
), we know that
\bar{w}_{+}(0)=\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
. So

\frac{\bar{w}_{\pm}(r)}{r}=\frac{\bar{w}_{\pm}(r)-\bar{w}_{\pm}(0)}{r}=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{r}(\bar{w}_{\pm})(\tau r)d\tau.

Then it follows from the fact that
\overline{U}_{\pm}(r)
are
C^{7}
-functions that
\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\in C^{6}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
. By (
5.49
), (
5.76
) and (
5.96
), we know that
{F}_{2}(z,r)\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
and

(5.97)

\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq C(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}).

It follows from (
5.49
) and
\delta\theta_{1}(z,0)=0
that
{F}_{2}(z,0)=0
.
Then

(5.98)

\displaystyle{F}^{\sharp}=\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}{F}_{2}(z,r)}{r}=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\frac{{F}_{2}(z,r)-{F}_{2}(z,0)}{r}.

So it follows from (
5.97
) that

(5.99)

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\|F^{\sharp}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\Omega_{+}^{K})}\\[5.69054pt]
\leq&C\|\partial_{r}F_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\Omega_{+}^{K})}\\[5.69054pt]
\leq&C(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}).\end{array}

Because some of the coefficients in equation (
5.95
) tend to infinity as
r\rightarrow 0
, we will consider the elliptic problem in five dimensions to remove the symmetry-axis singularity.
Let
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})
and
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{4}r_{i}^{2}=r^{2}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}:=\{(z,\mathbf{r}):z\in(K,L),\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{4},|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}\}.

Let

\mathbf{Z}:=(z,\mathbf{r})\in\mathcal{D}.

Then obviously
{F}^{\sharp}(z,r)\in C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})
and satisfies

(5.100)

\displaystyle\|F^{\sharp}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq C(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}).

Let
\psi(z,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=\Psi(z,\sqrt{r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}+r_{3}^{2}+r_{4}^{2}})
,
then
\llbracket\textit{Problem II}\rrbracket
can be rewritten as the following
\llbracket\textit{Problem II(5D)}\rrbracket
.
Equation (
5.95
) becomes

(5.101)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\psi\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r_{i}}\psi\Big)+F_{0}(r)\psi={F}^{\sharp}(z,\mathbf{r})\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{D},

where

(5.102)

\displaystyle F_{0}(r):=\frac{2}{r}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\Big)-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}.

Boundary condition (
5.92
) becomes

(5.103)

\displaystyle\psi=0\qquad\text{on}\quad\partial\mathcal{D}.

In order to apply Lemma
4.1
to know that
F_{0}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})
, we need to show
\partial_{r}F_{0}(0)=0
. Let

(5.104)

\displaystyle F_{0}(r):=\overline{F}_{1}(r)+\overline{F}_{2}(r),

where
\overline{F}_{1}(r):=\frac{2}{r}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\Big)
and
\overline{F}_{2}(r):=-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}
.
Direct calculations yield that

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overline{F}_{1}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Big(-\frac{2}{\gamma}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}-1}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{r})+\frac{\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{\rho}_{+}}{r})+\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{+}}{r})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{(1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2})^{2}}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{r})\Big)

\displaystyle+2\Big(-\frac{2}{\gamma}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{r})\partial_{r}\big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}-1}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\big)+(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{\rho}_{+}}{r})\partial_{r}\big(\frac{\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\big)+(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{+}}{r})\partial_{r}\big(\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\big)

(5.105)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\frac{\partial_{r}\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{r})\partial_{r}\big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{(1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2})^{2}}\big)\Big)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overline{F}_{2}(r)=

\displaystyle-\big(\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\partial_{r}\bar{\rho}_{+}+\bar{\rho}_{+}\partial_{r}(\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r})\big)\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{w}_{+}+\partial_{r}(\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r})\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}

(5.106)

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\gamma}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}-1}\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}.

By applying
\bar{w}_{+}(0)=\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

(5.107)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overline{F}_{2}(0)=0.

In (
5.105
),

(5.108)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{r})=\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{r\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}-\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{r^{2}}=\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{r\partial_{r}^{3}\bar{p}_{+}}{2r}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{3}\bar{p}_{+}(0).

Similarly,

(5.109)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{\rho}_{+}}{r})=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{3}\bar{\rho}_{+}(0),\,\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{+}}{r})=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{3}\bar{q}_{+}(0),\,\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\partial_{r}(\frac{\partial_{r}\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{r})=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{3}\overline{M}_{+}^{2}(0).

Then it follows from Lemma
3.1
that

(5.110)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\overline{F}_{1}(0)=0.

Thus

\partial_{r}F_{0}(0)=\partial_{r}\overline{F}_{1}(0)+\partial_{r}\overline{F}_{2}(0)=0
. Therefore,

(5.111)

F_{0},\,\mbox{which only depends on the special shock solution, is in }C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}}).

Now the well-posedness of problem (
5.101
)-(
5.103
) and then the well-posedness of problem (
5.92
) and (
5.95
) will be established by proving the following lemma.

Lemma 5.5
.

There exists a positive constant
r_{*}
, only depending on
\overline{U}_{-}(r)
and
\gamma
, such that for
0<r_{0}<r_{*}
, there exists a unique weak solution
\psi\in H_{0}^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})
to problem (
5.101
)-(
5.103
). Moreover, if (
5.49
) holds, the
H_{0}^{1}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})
weak solution
\psi
of the
\llbracket\textit{Problem II(5D)}\rrbracket
is in
C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})
and satisfies

(5.112)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}\Big(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta\theta_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
,
L
and
\alpha
. Moreover,

(5.113)

\psi(z,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})\equiv\Psi(z,\sqrt{r_{1}^{2}+r_{2}^{2}+r_{3}^{2}+r_{4}^{2}}),

where
\Psi\in C^{2,\alpha}(\Omega_{+}^{K})
is a solution of
\llbracket\textit{Problem II}\rrbracket
, and it holds that

\displaystyle\|\delta p_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta\theta_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}

(5.114)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
K
,
L
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
and
\alpha
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into four steps.

Step 1.
In this step, we will prove the uniqueness of the solution
\psi
. Assume that
\psi_{1}
and
\psi_{2}
are two solutions to problem (
5.101
)-(
5.103
). Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}:=\psi_{1}-\psi_{2}.

Then
\widetilde{\psi}
satisfies the following equation

(5.115)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\widetilde{\psi}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\bar{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r_{i}}\widetilde{\psi}\Big)+F_{0}(r)\widetilde{\psi}=0\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{D},

with the boundary condition

(5.116)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}=0\qquad\text{on}\quad\partial\mathcal{D}.

Multiplying
\widetilde{\psi}
on the both sides of equation (
5.115
), integrating over
\mathcal{D}
and then integration by part, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}}{F}_{0}(r)\widetilde{\psi}^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}}\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}|\partial_{z}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\int_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\bar{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}|\partial_{r_{i}}\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}\{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\}\int_{\mathcal{D}}|D\widetilde{\psi}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}

(5.117)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}\{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\}\frac{1}{4r_{0}^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{D}}\widetilde{\psi}^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z},

where the last inequality is by Poincaré’s inequality (see
[
21
, Proposition 3.10]
).
So if we can show

(5.118)

\displaystyle\max\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}{F}_{0}(r)\leq\frac{1}{4r_{0}^{2}}\min\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}\{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\},

(
5.117
) yields that
\widetilde{\psi}\equiv 0
. Then the uniqueness of the solutions
\psi
follows.

Hence, we remains to show (
5.118
) in this step. It follows from (
2.26
)-(
2.31
) and (
5.104
) that

\displaystyle{F}_{0}(r)=

\displaystyle\frac{2\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r^{2}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\gamma-1+2t}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\frac{\gamma-1+2t}{1-t}\cdot\frac{(\gamma-1)(\gamma+t)+(\gamma-2)(\gamma+1)}{\gamma+1}

\displaystyle\qquad+(\gamma-1)(\gamma+t)+(\gamma+1)+(\gamma+1)\frac{r\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}}{\bar{w}_{-}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\gamma+1}{2}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\Big((\gamma-1)-\frac{(\gamma-1)(\gamma+1)^{2}(1-t)^{2}}{(\gamma-1+2t)^{2}(2\gamma-(\gamma-1)t)}\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle 2\frac{\gamma+1}{\gamma-1}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{r^{2}}\Big(\frac{2(\gamma-1)}{1-t}+\gamma+\frac{r\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}}{\bar{w}_{-}}+\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\Big),

where
t(r)=\frac{1}{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}(r)}
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}=\frac{\gamma-1+2t}{\gamma+1}\Big(\frac{2\gamma\overline{M}_{-}^{2}-\gamma+1}{\gamma+1}\bar{p}_{-}\Big)^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}.

Obviously, there exists a constant
r_{*}>0
such that

(5.119)

r_{*}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\mu^{2}\min\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}\left\{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{-}}\bar{q}_{-}\Big(\frac{2\gamma\overline{M}_{-}^{2}-\gamma+1}{\gamma+1}\bar{p}_{-}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\right\}}{4\max\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}\left\{\frac{\bar{w}_{-}\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{-}}\Big(\frac{2\gamma\overline{M}_{-}^{2}-\gamma+1}{\gamma+1}\bar{p}_{-}\Big)^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{2(\gamma-1)}{1-t}+\gamma+\frac{r\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}}{\bar{w}_{-}}+\frac{\gamma-1}{2}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}}\right\}}>0.

Then for any
0<r_{0}\leq r_{*}
, by a straightforward calculation, we know that (
5.118
) holds.

Step 2.
In this step, we will prove the existence of the weak solution
\psi\in H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})
to
\llbracket\textit{Problem II(5D)}\rrbracket
.
Let
\zeta\in H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})
be a test function. The bilinear form corresponding to problem
\llbracket\textit{Problem II(5D)}\rrbracket
is as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[\psi,\zeta]:=\int_{\mathcal{D}}\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\psi\partial_{z}\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\int_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\bar{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r_{i}}\psi\partial_{r_{i}}\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}-\int_{\mathcal{D}}F_{0}(r)\psi\zeta\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}.

It is easy to check that
\mathcal{B}[\psi,\zeta]
is bounded. By applying (
5.117
) and (
5.118
), we obtain for any
0<r_{0}<r_{*}
,

(5.120)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}[\psi,\psi]\geq\beta\|\psi\|_{H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})}^{2},

where the constant
\beta>0
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
,
L
and
K
. By the Lax-Milgram theorem, there exists a unique solution
\psi\in H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})
to problem
\llbracket\textit{Problem II(5D)}\rrbracket
.

Next, we will establish an
H^{1}
-estimate of the solution
\psi
. Note that
\psi\in H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})
is a weak solution of problem
\llbracket\textit{Problem II(5D)}\rrbracket
if
\mathcal{B}[\psi,\zeta]=\int_{\mathcal{D}}{F}^{\sharp}\psi\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}
for any test function
\zeta\in H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})
. Choosing
\zeta=\psi
, we have

(5.121)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{D}}\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}|\partial_{z}\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\int_{\mathcal{D}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\bar{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}|\partial_{r_{i}}\psi|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}-\int_{\mathcal{D}}{F}_{0}\psi^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}=\int_{\mathcal{D}}{F}^{\sharp}\psi\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Z}.

By applying Cauchy’s inequality with small constant
\epsilon>0
, (
5.121
) implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4r_{0}^{2}}\min\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}\{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})}^{2}-\max\limits_{|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}}{F}_{0}(r)\|\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})}^{2}\leq\epsilon\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\epsilon}\|{F}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})}^{2}.

Then it follows from (
5.118
) and the Poincaré’s inequality that

(5.122)

\|\psi\|_{H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{D})}\leq{C}\|{F}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})},

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
and
L
.

By (
5.99
) we know that

\|{F}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{D})}\leq C(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}).

Therefore, by (
5.122
), we have

(5.123)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{H_{0}^{1}({\mathcal{D}})}\leq C(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
,
L
and
\alpha
.

Step 3.
In this step, we will prove estimate (
5.112
). Let set
\{(z,r)\}:=\{(z,\mathbf{r})\in\overline{\mathcal{D}}:\,|\mathbf{r}|=r\}
.
It follows from the local boundary estimate
[
22
, Corollary 8.36]
and interior estimate
[
22
, Theorem 8.32]
with
g=0
, there exists
\alpha\in(0,1)
such that for any
\Omega^{\prime}\Subset\overline{\mathcal{D}}\backslash\{(K,r_{0}),\,(L,r_{0})\}
,

(5.124)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq{C}\|\psi\|_{C^{0}({\mathcal{D}})}+\|{F}^{\sharp}\|_{L^{q}(\mathcal{D})}),

where
\alpha=1-n/q
and constant
C
depends on
\Omega^{\prime}
but does not depend on
\psi
.

It remains to consider the
C^{1,\alpha}
-estimate of solution
\psi
near the points in the set
\{(K,r_{0}),\,(L,r_{0})\}
.
Without loss of the generality, we only consider the regularity of
\psi
near the set
\{(K,r_{0})\}
.

Since
\psi(z,\mathbf{r})\big|_{|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}}=0
,
f_{2}(z,r_{0})=0
and
\delta\theta_{1}(z,r_{0})=0
, we define

\displaystyle\psi^{\star}(z,\mathbf{r}):=\begin{cases}\psi(z,\mathbf{r})\quad&0<|\mathbf{r}|\leq r_{0}\\
-\psi(z,\mathbf{r}^{\star})\quad&|\mathbf{r}|>r_{0}\end{cases}\qquad{F}_{2}^{\star}(z,r):=\begin{cases}{F}_{2}(z,r)\quad&0<r<r_{0}\\
-{F}_{2}(z,2r_{0}-r)\quad&r\geq r_{0}\end{cases}

where
\mathbf{r}^{\star}
is the reflection point of
\mathbf{r}
with respect to the nozzle wall
|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}
, such that the segment
\mathbf{r}\mathbf{r}^{\star}
is perpendicular to the nozzle wall and the distances from points
\mathbf{r}
and
\mathbf{r}^{\star}
to the nozzle wall are equal.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{\tau}
be a small domain in
\mathcal{D}
with
\{(K,r_{0})\}\subset\overline{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}
. Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}:=\mathcal{D}_{\tau}\cup\big\{(z,\mathbf{r}^{\star}):(z,\mathbf{r})\in\mathcal{D}_{\tau}\big\}\cup(\big\{(z,\mathbf{r}):\,|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}\big\}\cap\overline{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}).

It follows from Lemma
3.1
that we can extend
\bar{p}_{+}
,
\bar{s}_{+}
and
\bar{M}^{2}_{+}
evenly across the boundary
\{|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}\}
, and then the same do for
\bar{\rho}_{+}
and
\bar{q}_{+}
from the thermal relations.
Then we easily know that
{\psi}^{\star}
satisfies the following equation

(5.125)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\psi^{\star}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r_{i}}\psi^{\star}\Big)+{F}_{0}(r)\psi^{\star}={F}^{\sharp}\qquad\text{in}\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}

where

{F}^{\sharp}
is defined by (
5.98
) by replacing
F_{2}
by
F_{2}^{\star}
.
Meanwhile, the boundary condition on
\Gamma:=\{z=K\}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}
is

(5.126)

\displaystyle\psi^{\star}=0\qquad\text{on}\quad\Gamma.

Then it follows from
[
22
, Corollary 8.36]
again that for any smooth domain
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}^{\sharp}\Subset\Gamma\cup\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}
, we have

(5.127)

\displaystyle\|\psi^{\star}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}}^{\sharp})}\leq C\Big(\|{F}^{\sharp}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi^{\star}\|_{C^{0}(\widetilde{\mathcal{D}_{\tau}})}\Big).

Therefore, by (
5.124
) and (
5.127
), and by the definition of
\psi^{\star}
and
F_{2}^{\star}
, we have

(5.128)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

Next, let us consider the
C^{2,\alpha}
-estimate of
\psi
. Take derivative on (
5.101
) with respect to
z
and denote

(5.129)

\displaystyle\psi^{z}(z,\mathbf{r}):=\partial_{z}\psi(z,\mathbf{r}),\,\,R_{1}(\mathbf{r}):=\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}},\,\,R_{2}(\mathbf{r}):=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}.

Because
\partial_{z}R_{i}=0
and by (
5.100
),
\psi^{z}
satisfies the following elliptic equation

(5.130)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(R_{1}(\mathbf{r})\partial_{z}\psi^{z}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(R_{2}(\mathbf{r})\partial_{r_{i}}\psi^{z}\Big)=\partial_{z}\big({F}^{\sharp}(z,r)\big)-F_{0}(\mathbf{r})\psi^{z}\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{D},

where
r=|\mathbf{r}|
.
Next, by direct calculation, it follows from (
5.101
) and (
5.103
) that
\psi^{z}
satisfies the following Neumann-Dirichlet mixed boundary conditions

(5.131)

\displaystyle\psi^{z}(z,\mathbf{r})\big|_{|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}}=0,\quad\partial_{z}\psi^{z}(K,\mathbf{r})=\frac{{F}^{\sharp}(K,\mathbf{r})}{R_{1}(\mathbf{r})},\quad\partial_{z}\psi^{z}(L,\mathbf{r})=\frac{{F}^{\sharp}(L,\mathbf{r})}{R_{1}(\mathbf{r})}.

Notice that by (
5.49
), (
5.56
), (
5.96
) and (
5.100
), we have

(5.132)

\displaystyle{F}^{\sharp}(z,r_{0})=\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(r_{0}){F}_{2}(z,r_{0})}{r_{0}}=0\qquad\text{for}\quad z\in[K,L].

Therefore, boundary conditions (
5.131
) are compatible.
So near the set
\{(K,r_{0}),\,(L,r_{0})\}
, we do an odd extension across boundary
|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}
for
\psi^{z}
, even extensions for
R_{i}
,
\partial_{z}\big({F}^{\sharp}\big)
and
F_{0}
, and apply
[
29
, Theorem 5.54]
near the boundary
z=K
or
z=L
for the conormal boundary conditions and
[
21
, Theorem 5.21]
interiorly, to obtain

(5.133)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}\psi\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

Then, we will prove that
\psi^{r_{j}}:=\partial_{r_{i}}\psi\in C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})
for
j=1,2,3,4
and for any subdomain
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\Subset(\overline{\mathcal{D}}\backslash\{|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}\})
. Taking derivatives on both sides of the equation (
5.101
) with respect to the variable
r_{j}
, we have
\psi^{r_{j}}
satisfies equation

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(R_{1}(\mathbf{r})\partial_{z}\psi^{r_{j}}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(R_{2}(\mathbf{r})\partial_{r_{i}}\psi^{r_{j}}\Big)

(5.134)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{r_{j}}\big({F}^{\sharp}(z,r)\big)-\partial_{z}\Big(\partial_{r_{j}}R_{1}(\mathbf{r})\partial_{z}\psi\Big)-\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(\partial_{r_{j}}R_{2}(\mathbf{r})\partial_{r_{i}}\psi\Big)-\partial_{r_{j}}\Big(F_{0}(\mathbf{r})\psi\Big)\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{D}.

For the boundary condition, it follows from (
5.103
) that

(5.135)

\psi^{r_{j}}(K,\mathbf{r})=\psi^{r_{j}}(L,\mathbf{r})=0.

Hence, it follows from
[
22
, Corollary 8.36]
and
[
22
, Theorem 8.32]
again that for any
j=1,2,3,4
and for any subdomain
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\Subset(\overline{\mathcal{D}}\backslash\{|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}\})

(5.136)

\|\partial_{r_{j}}\psi\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

Finally, let us consider the estimate near the nozzle wall
|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{D}_{r}=\mathcal{D}\cap\{(z,\mathbf{r}),\,|\mathbf{r}|\geq r\}
. For any subdomain
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\Subset\overline{\mathcal{D}}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{D}_{r_{0}/2}}
, consider tangential derivative to the boundary of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
,
\partial_{\tau}=r_{i}\partial_{r_{j}}-r_{j}\partial_{r_{i}}
, where
i,j=1,\cdots,4
with
i\neq j
.
It follows from boundary condition (
5.103
) that

(5.137)

\partial_{\tau}\psi=0\qquad\mbox{on }\partial\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\cap\overline{\mathcal{D}_{r_{0}/2}}.

Then similarly, by following the argument to derive the equation for
\partial_{\tau}\psi
in divergence form and the even/odd extension as before, based on the Dirichlet boundary condition (
5.137
), and by
[
22
, Corollary 8.36]
, we can have for
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\Subset\overline{\mathcal{D}}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{D}_{r_{0}/2}}
and for
i,j=1,\cdots,4
with
i\neq j
,

(5.138)

\|(r_{i}\partial_{r_{j}}-r_{j}\partial_{r_{i}})\psi\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

Let
\partial_{\tau_{ij}}=(r_{i}\partial_{r_{j}}-r_{j}\partial_{r_{i}})
and
\partial_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}r_{i}\partial_{r_{i}}
. We have

(5.139)

\psi_{r_{i}}=\frac{r_{i}\psi_{n}-\sum_{j\neq i}r_{j}\psi_{\tau_{ij}}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}},

and then

(5.140)

\sum_{i=1}^{4}\psi_{r_{i}r_{i}}=\frac{\psi_{nn}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}(\frac{r_{i}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}})_{r_{i}}\psi_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{4}(\frac{\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{\tau_{ij}}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}})_{r_{i}}.

By (
5.129
), we know that
R_{2}\geq C>0
. It follows from equation (
5.125
) that

\displaystyle\psi_{nn}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}}{R_{2}}\left({F}^{\sharp}-R_{1}\psi_{z}z-{F}_{0}(r)\psi\right)-|\mathbf{r}|^{2}(\sum_{i=1}^{4}(\frac{r_{i}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}})_{r_{i}}\psi_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{4}(\frac{\sum_{j\neq i}\psi_{\tau_{ij}}}{|\mathbf{r}|^{2}})_{r_{i}}).

Because
|\mathbf{r}|\geq r_{0}/2>0
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
, it follows from estimates (
5.128
), (
5.133
) and (
5.138
) that

(5.141)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{nn}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

So for any
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\Subset\overline{\mathcal{D}}\backslash\overline{\mathcal{D}_{r_{0}/2}}
,

(5.142)

\|\psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\mathcal{D}^{\prime})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

It further with estimate (
5.136
), we have

(5.143)

\|\psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}(\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}+\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}).

Finally, we will show the following estimate from (
5.143
)

(5.144)

\displaystyle\|\psi\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}.

In fact, if it is not true, then for any
k>0
, there exists
\psi_{k}
and
F^{k}_{2}
with
\|\psi_{k}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}=1
such that
1=\|\psi_{k}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}>k\|{F}^{k}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}
. Then

(5.145)

F_{2}^{k}\rightarrow 0\qquad\mbox{as }k\rightarrow\infty\quad\mbox{in }C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}}).

Moreover, it follows from (
5.143
) that
\|\psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}(\frac{1}{k}+1)
. So there exists a subsequence which we still denote as
\psi_{k}
such that

(5.146)

\psi_{k}\rightarrow\psi^{*}\qquad\mbox{as }k\rightarrow\infty\quad\mbox{in }C^{2}(\overline{\mathcal{D}}).

So it follows from (
5.98
), (
5.101
), (
5.145
) and (
5.146
) that
\psi^{*}
satisfies the following boundary value problem

(5.147)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\psi^{*}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r_{i}}\psi^{*}\Big)+F_{0}(r)\psi^{*}=0\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{D}

and

(5.148)

\psi^{*}=0\qquad\mbox{on }\partial\mathcal{D}.

By the uniqueness obtained in step 1, we know that
\psi^{*}\equiv 0
. It is contradicts to
1=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\|\psi_{k}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}=\|\psi^{*}\|_{C^{0}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}
.

Hence, (
5.142
) and (
5.144
) yield

(5.149)

\|\psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})}\leq{C}\|{F}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\mathcal{D}})},

and then (
5.112
) follows.

Step 4.
In this step, we will prove (
5.113
) and (
5.114
).
First, it is easy to check that the problem (
5.101
)-(
5.103
) is rotationally invariant. Thus, (
5.113
) follows from the uniqueness of the solutions.
Then it follows from (
5.112
) and Lemma
4.1
that
\Psi
satisfies (
5.92
), (
5.93
), (
5.95
), and estimate

(5.150)

\displaystyle\|\Psi\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq{C}\Big(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta\theta_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
\gamma
,
r_{0}
,
L
,
K
and
\alpha
.
Applying (
5.150
), for
\widehat{\Psi}=r^{2}\Psi
, we have

(5.151)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\Psi}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq{C}_{+}\Big(\|f_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta\theta_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\Big).

Define
(\delta{\theta}_{2},\delta{p}_{2})
by (
5.90
). Then (
5.114
) follows from (
5.76
) and (
5.151
).
∎

To conclude the proof of Theorem
5.3
, we need to further raise the regularity of
\delta p
and
\delta\theta
, which is the following lemma.

Lemma 5.6
.

Assume the assumptions in Theorem
5.3
hold. Let
(\delta p,\delta\theta)=(\delta p_{1}+\delta p_{2},\delta\theta_{1}+\delta\theta_{2})
, where
(\delta p_{1},\delta\theta_{1})
and
(\delta p_{2},\delta\theta_{2})
are obtained by Lemma
5.4
and Lemma
5.5
, respectively. Then
(\delta p,\delta\theta)\in{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}
and satisfies

(5.152)

\displaystyle\|\delta p\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq C\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
K
,
L
,
r_{0}
and
\alpha
.
Moreover, it holds that

(5.153)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\delta p(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}\delta p(z,r_{0})=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{2}\delta\theta(z,0)=0.

Proof.

This proof is divided into two steps.

Step 1:
In this step, we will prove the
C^{2,\alpha}
regularity of
\delta p
.
Let

(5.154)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(r):=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{+}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{+}+\frac{\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\big)\Big).

It follows from (
5.43
) and (
5.44
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta p\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta p\big)\Big)-\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\frac{\partial_{z}\delta\theta}{r}

(5.155)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}f_{1}+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\mathcal{A}(r)\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau\Big).

By employing (
5.44
), it holds that

(5.156)

\displaystyle-\partial_{z}\delta\theta=\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\big)-\mathcal{A}(r)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau-\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}\Big).

Substituting (
5.156
) into (
5.155
), one has

(5.157)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}^{2}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta p\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta p\big)\Big)+\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta p\big)={F}(z,r)

where

\displaystyle{F}(z,r):=

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}f_{1}+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}\Big)+\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}

\displaystyle+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\mathcal{A}(r)\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)+\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\mathcal{A}(r)\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau.

The boundary conditions of
\delta p
on
z=K
and
z=L
are

(5.158)

\displaystyle\delta p(K,r)=g_{1}(K,r)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\delta p(L,r)=\sigma p_{ex}(L,r).

For the boundary conditions on the nozzle wall and the symmetry axis, it follows from
f_{2}(z,0)=f_{2}(z,r_{0})=0
,
\delta\theta(z,0)=\delta(z,r_{0})=0
,
\delta w(z,0)=0
,
\bar{w}_{+}(0)=\bar{w}(r_{0})=0
, and (
5.44
) that

(5.159)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\delta p(z,0)=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\partial_{r}\delta p(z,r_{0})=0.

Obviously, the mixed boundary problem (
5.157
)-(
5.159
) has singularity near the axis
r=0
.
Similarly as done for
\llbracket\textit{Problem I}\rrbracket
,
let
\delta P(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=\delta p(x_{1},\sqrt{x_{2}^{2}+x_{3}^{2}})
. By boundary condition (
5.159
) and Lemma
4.1
,
\delta P\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathcal{P})
if and only if
\delta p\in C^{2,\alpha}(\Omega^{K}_{+})
.
Then problem (
5.157
)-(
5.159
) can be rewritten as the following problem:

(5.160)

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{1}}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{x_{1}}\delta P\Big)+\sum_{i=2}^{3}\partial_{x_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{2}{\gamma}}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{x_{i}}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta P\big)\Big)={F}(x_{1},r)\qquad\text{in}\quad\mathcal{P}

with the boundary conditions:

(5.161)

\displaystyle\delta P(K,x_{2},x_{3})=g_{1}(K,r)

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{K}

(5.162)

\displaystyle\delta P(L,x_{2},x_{3})=\sigma p_{ex}(r)

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{ex}

(5.163)

\displaystyle x_{2}\partial_{x_{2}}\delta P(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})+x_{3}\partial_{x_{3}}\phi(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=0

on

\displaystyle\quad\mathcal{P}_{w}.

By
\partial_{r}g_{1}(K,0)=\partial_{r}g_{1}(K,r_{0})=0
and
\partial_{r}p_{ex}(0)=\partial_{r}p_{ex}(r_{0})=0
, it is easy to see that
g_{1}
and
p_{ex}
are
C^{2,\alpha}
functions in
\mathcal{P}_{K}
and
\mathcal{P}_{ex}
respecitvely. Because

\|F\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\mathcal{P})}\leq C\|F\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq C\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big),

following a similar argumant as given in the proof of Lemma
5.4
, we can obtain

(5.164)

\displaystyle\|\delta p\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq C\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big).

Step 2:
In this step, we will estimate
\delta\theta
. Applying (
5.44
), we have

(5.165)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\delta\theta=-\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\big)-\mathcal{A}(r)\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{K}^{z}\delta{\theta}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau-\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{2}\Big)\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}}),

where we use the facts that
\delta p\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
,
\delta\theta\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
and
f_{2}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
.
Next, by equation (
5.43
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{\theta}\big)=\partial_{z}\big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\big)-r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}.

So it follows from the condition
\delta\theta(z,0)=0
that

\displaystyle\delta{\theta}(z,r)=\frac{\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\int_{0}^{r}\tau\Big(\partial_{z}\big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\big)-\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}\Big)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Direct calculations yield that

(5.166)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\delta{\theta}(z,r)=

\displaystyle J_{1}+J_{2},

where
J_{1}:=\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}J_{*}
,
