J_{2}:=(\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\big)-\frac{1}{r^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}})\int_{0}^{r}\tau J_{*}(z,\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau=(r\partial_{r}\big(\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\big)-\bar{p}_{+}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}})\int_{0}^{1}\tau J_{*}(z,r\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,

and

J_{*}:=\partial_{z}\big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\delta{p}\big)-\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}.

By
\delta p\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
and
f_{1}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
, it is easy to see that
J_{*}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
. Hence

J_{1}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
,

J_{2}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})
,
and it holds that

(5.167)

\displaystyle\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}\leq C\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|g_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\Big).

Finally, by equation (
5.43
), we know that

\partial_{r}\delta\theta=\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}^{2}_{+}}\partial_{z}\delta p-(\frac{1}{r}+\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{\gamma\bar{p}_{+}})\delta\theta-f_{1}.

So

(5.168)

\partial_{r}^{2}\delta\theta+\partial_{r}(\frac{\delta\theta}{r})=\partial_{r}\left(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}^{2}_{+}}\partial_{z}\delta p-\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{\gamma\bar{p}_{+}}\delta\theta-f_{1}\right).

Because
\partial_{r}\overline{M}_{+}(0)=\partial_{r}\bar{\rho}_{+}(0)=\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}(0)=\delta\theta(z,0)=\partial_{r}\delta p(z,0)=\partial_{r}f_{1}(z,0)=0
, it is easy to see that

\partial_{r}\left(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}^{2}_{+}}\partial_{z}\delta p-\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{p}_{+}}{\gamma\bar{p}_{+}}\delta\theta-f_{1}\right)(z,0)=0.

Finally, notice that

\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\partial_{r}(\frac{\delta\theta}{r})=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{r\partial_{r}\delta\theta-\delta\theta}{r^{2}}=\lim_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{r\partial_{r}^{2}\delta\theta}{2r}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{2}\delta\theta(z,0).

Then it follows from (
5.168
) that
\partial_{r}^{2}\delta\theta(z,0)=0
.
∎

Therefore, Theorem
5.3
follows from Lemma
5.4
, Lemma
5.5
and Lemma
5.6
.

5.3.
Cauchy problem for the three linearized transport equations

Once
\delta\theta
is determined, we will solve
(\delta w,\delta q,\delta s)
by considering the following Cauchy problems in the subsonic domain
\Omega_{+}^{K}
.

(5.169)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)(r\delta w)+\partial_{r}(r\bar{w}_{+})\cdot\delta\theta={f}_{3}(z,r)&\text{in}\quad{\Omega}_{+}^{K},\\[3.0pt]
\delta w(K,r)=g_{2}(K,r);\end{cases}

(5.170)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)\delta B+\partial_{r}\bar{B}_{+}\cdot\delta\theta=f_{4}(z,r)&\text{in}\quad{\Omega}_{+}^{K},\\[3.0pt]
\delta q(K,r)=g_{3}(K,r);\end{cases}

(5.171)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)\delta s+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}\delta\theta={f}_{5}(z,r)&\text{in}\quad{\Omega}_{+}^{K},\\[3.0pt]
\delta s(K,r)=g_{4}(K,r).\end{cases}

The slop of the shock front
\delta{\varphi}^{\prime}
can be easily determined by equation

(5.172)

\displaystyle\delta{\varphi}^{\prime}=g_{5}(K,r).

We further assume that

(5.173)

\displaystyle(f_{i},H)\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}}),(i=3,4,5),\quad g_{j}\in C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}]),(j=2,3,4,5),

(5.174)

\displaystyle{f}_{3}(z,0)={f}_{3}(z,r_{0})=0,\quad H(z,0)=H(z,r_{0})=0,

(5.175)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}f_{j}(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}f_{j}(z,r_{0})=0,(j=4,5),

(5.176)

\displaystyle g_{2}(K,0)=0,\quad g_{2}(K,r_{0})=0,

(5.177)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}g_{j}(K,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}g_{j}(K,r_{0})=0,(j=3,4),

(5.178)

\displaystyle g_{5}(K,0)=0,\quad g_{5}(K,r_{0})=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{2}g_{5}(K,0)=0.

By the method of characteristics, we can obtain the existence of the solution
(\delta w,\delta q,\delta s)
for the Cauchy problems (
5.169
)-(
5.171
) as follows.

Theorem 5.7
.

Assume the conditions (
5.173
)-(
5.178
) hold, then the Cauchy problems (
5.169
)-(
5.171
) admit a unique solution

(r\delta w,\delta q,\delta s)\in C^{2,\alpha}({\Omega}_{+}^{K})
with
\delta w\in C^{1,\alpha}({\Omega}_{+}^{K})
,
which satisfy

\displaystyle\|(r\delta w,\delta q,\delta s)\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}+\|\delta w\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega_{+}^{K}})}

(5.179)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\Omega_{+}^{K})}+\sum_{i=3}^{5}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\Omega_{+}^{K})}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\|g_{i}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}+\|\sigma p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\right),

where constant
C
depends only on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
H
,
K
,
L
,
r_{0}
and
\alpha
.
Moreover, it holds that

(5.180)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}(\delta q)(z,0)=\partial_{r}(\delta s)(z,0)=\partial_{r}(\delta q)(z,r_{0})=\partial_{r}(\delta s)(z,r_{0})=\delta w(z,r_{0})=0

provided that
\delta w(K,r_{0})=\partial_{r}\delta{s}(K,0)=\partial_{r}\delta s(K,r_{0})=\partial_{r}\delta q(K,0)=\partial_{r}\delta q(K,r_{0})=0
.
For the shock front
\delta\varphi
satisfying (
5.172
), it holds that

(5.181)

\displaystyle\|\delta\varphi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}\leq\|g_{5}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}([0,r_{0}])}

and

(5.182)

\displaystyle\delta\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\delta\varphi^{(3)}(0)=\delta\varphi^{\prime}(r_{0})=0.

Proof.

Note that (
5.39
) and (
5.40
) are the solution experssions of problems (
5.169
) and (
5.171
) respectively.
Moreover, it follows from (
5.170
) that

\displaystyle\delta B(z,r)=

\displaystyle B(R(K;z,{r}))+\int_{K}^{z}{f}_{4}(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau

(5.183)

\displaystyle-\int_{K}^{z}\partial_{r}\bar{B}_{+}(R(\tau;z,{r}))\delta\theta(\tau,R(\tau;z,{r}))\mathrm{d}\tau.

So estimate (
5.179
) follows immediately. Here we use the fact that
W(z,0)=0
for
W=r\delta w
such that

\delta w(z,r)=\frac{W(z,r)-W(z,0)}{r}=\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{r}W(z,\tau r)d\tau.

Since
H(z,r_{0})=\delta\theta(z,r_{0})={f}_{3}(z,r_{0})=0
, it follows from
(
5.169
) that
\partial_{z}\delta w=0
on the boundary
r=r_{0}
. Therefore,
\delta w(z,r_{0})=0
provided that
\delta w(K,r_{0})=0.

Taking derivative with respect to the variable
r
on the both sides of the equation (
5.171
), we have

\displaystyle\big(\partial_{z}+H(z,r)\partial_{r}\big)(\partial_{r}\delta{s})+\partial_{r}H(z,r)\partial_{r}\delta{s}+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}\partial_{r}\delta{\theta}+\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{s}_{+}\delta{\theta}=\partial_{r}f_{5}.

By applying
H(z,0)=0
,
\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{+}(0)=0
,
\delta{\theta}(z,0)=0
and
\partial_{r}f_{5}(z,0)=0
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(\partial_{r}\delta{s})(z,0)+\partial_{r}H(z,0)\partial_{r}\delta{s}(z,0)=0.

Thus,
\partial_{r}\delta{s}(z,0)=0
provided that
\partial_{r}\delta{s}(K,0)=0
.

Similarly, we can show
\partial_{r}\delta s(z,r_{0})=0
and

\partial_{r}\delta q(z,0)=\partial_{r}\delta q(z,r_{0})=0
, provided that
\partial_{r}\delta s(K,r_{0})=\partial_{r}\delta q(K,0)=\partial_{r}\delta q(K,r_{0})=0
.

For the shock front, (
5.181
) and (
5.182
) follow from (
5.172
) and (
5.178
).
∎

6.
Existence of shock solutions to the nonlinear problem

Based on Section 4 and Section 5, we will design a delicate nonlinear iteration scheme such that (
5.50
)-(
5.51
) and (
5.173
)-(
5.178
) hold to solve the nonlinear boundary value problem (
5.13
)-(
5.28
).
Then we can apply the Banach fixed-point theorem.
Before doing so, in order to determine the shock front position approximately based on the solvability condition (
5.52
), we will construct approximate shock solutions, around which the iteration scheme will be introduced.

6.1.
Approximate shock solutions

Let
\dot{U}_{-}=(\dot{p}_{-},\dot{\theta}_{-},\dot{w}_{-},\dot{q}_{-},\dot{s}_{-})^{\top}
be defined in
\dot{\Omega}_{-}:=\{(z,r)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:0<z<\dot{z}_{*},\,0<r<r_{0}\}
and satisfy the following linearized Euler equations:

(6.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-}\Big)-\Big(\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}+\frac{1}{r}\big(1+\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\big)\dot{\theta}_{-}\Big)=0

(6.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-})+\partial_{r}\dot{p}_{-}-\frac{1}{r}\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-}-\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\dot{w}_{-}+\frac{1}{\gamma c_{v}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r}\dot{s}_{-}=0

(6.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(r\dot{w}_{-})+\partial_{r}(r\bar{w}_{-})\cdot\dot{\theta}_{-}=0

(6.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\big(\dot{p}_{-}+\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}\dot{q}_{-}\big)+\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{-}\cdot\dot{\theta}_{-}=0

(6.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\dot{s}_{-}+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}\cdot\dot{\theta}_{-}=0

with initial-boundary conditions:

(6.6)

\displaystyle\dot{U}_{-}=(0,0,\sigma w_{en}(r),\sigma q_{en}(r),0)

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad\Gamma_{en}

(6.7)

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}_{-}=0

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad\Gamma_{w}\cap\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{-}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}_{-}=0\quad\dot{w}_{-}=0

\displaystyle\text{on}\quad\Gamma_{a}\cap\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{-}}.

Let
\dot{U}_{+}=(\dot{p}_{+},\dot{\theta}_{+},\dot{w}_{+},\dot{q}_{+},\dot{s}_{+})
satisfy (
5.13
), (
5.15
)-(
5.17
), (
5.24
)-(
5.28
) and (
5.41
) with

(6.9)

\displaystyle K:=\dot{z}_{*},\,\,H=0

and

(6.10)

\displaystyle f_{j}:=0,(j=1,3,4,5),\,\,f_{2}:=\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}}{r}\dot{w}_{+}(\dot{z}_{*},r)-\frac{1}{\gamma c_{v}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{w}_{+}^{2}}{r}\dot{s}_{+}(\dot{z}_{*},r).

Then condition (
5.52
) in Theorem
5.3
become

(6.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{p}_{+}(\dot{z}_{*},r)\mathrm{d}r=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\sigma p_{ex}(r)\mathrm{d}r.

Notice that
\dot{p}_{+}(\dot{z}_{*},r)
is an unknown quantity in (
6.11
), which can be determined by the supersonic solution
\dot{U}_{-}
ahead of the shock front and the R.-H. conditions (
5.27
)-(
5.28
), which can be rewritten as on
\dot{\Gamma}_{s}:=\{(z,r):z=\dot{z}_{*},\,0<r<r_{0}\}
,

(6.12)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{j}^{+}\cdot{\dot{U}}_{+}=\dot{G}_{j}:={\mathbf{\alpha}}_{j}^{-}\cdot{\dot{U}}_{-},\quad j=1,2,3,4,

(6.13)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{5}^{+}\cdot{\dot{U}}_{+}-[\bar{p}]{\dot{\varphi}}^{\prime}={\mathbf{\alpha}}_{5}^{-}\cdot{\dot{U}}_{-},

where
\alpha_{j}^{+}
are given in (
5.31
)-(
5.33
) and

(6.14)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{1}^{-}=\Big(\frac{\bar{q}_{-}}{\bar{c}_{-}^{2}},\,0,\,0,\,\bar{\rho}_{-},\,-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}}{\gamma c_{v}}\Big)^{\top},

(6.15)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{2}^{-}=\Big(1+\overline{M}_{-}^{2},\,0,\,0,\,2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-},\,-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}{\gamma c_{v}}\Big)^{\top},\quad{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{3}^{-}=\Big(0,\,0,\,1,\,0,\,0\Big)^{\top},

(6.16)

\displaystyle{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{4}^{-}=\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{-}},\,0,\,0,\,\bar{q}_{-},\,\frac{\bar{p}_{-}}{(\gamma-1)c_{v}\bar{\rho}_{-}}\Big)^{\top},\quad{\mathbf{\alpha}}_{5}^{-}=\Big(0,\,\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2},\,0,\,0,\,0\Big)^{\top}.

Following the argument in (
5.34
)-(
5.36
), we can solve (
6.12
) and (
6.13
) so that

(6.17)

\displaystyle\big(\dot{p}_{+},\,\dot{w}_{+},\,\dot{q}_{+},\,\dot{s}_{+}\big)(\dot{z}_{*},r)=\big(\dot{g}_{1}(r),\,\dot{g}_{2}(r),\,\dot{g}_{3}(r),\,\dot{g}_{4}(r)\big):=A_{s}^{-1}\dot{\mathbf{G}},

(6.18)

\displaystyle\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{+}-\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}}{[\bar{p}]}:=\dot{g}_{5}(r),

where
\dot{\mathbf{G}}=(\dot{G}_{1},\dot{G}_{2},\dot{G}_{3},\dot{G}_{4})
, and

(6.19)

\displaystyle\dot{g}_{1}(r)=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}-1}\Big(\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}-1}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}(1-\kappa_{1}(r))\dot{p}_{-}+\kappa_{2}(r)\sigma q_{en}(r)+\kappa_{3}(r)\int_{0}^{\dot{z}_{*}}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}z\Big),

(6.20)

\displaystyle\dot{g}_{2}(r)=

\displaystyle\sigma w_{en}(r)-\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\int_{0}^{\dot{z}_{*}}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle\dot{g}_{3}(r)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}}\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}-1}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\Big([\bar{p}]-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}-1}(1-\kappa_{1}(r))\Big)\dot{p}_{-}

\displaystyle+\Big(1+\frac{[\bar{p}]}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}-\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}-1}\kappa_{2}(r)\Big)\sigma q_{en}(r)

(6.21)

\displaystyle+\Big(\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}}{\gamma c_{v}}-\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{-}}{\bar{q}_{-}}\Big)\frac{[\bar{p}]}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}}-\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{-}-\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}-1}\kappa_{3}(r)\Big)\int_{0}^{\dot{z}_{*}}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}z,

\displaystyle\dot{g}_{4}(r)=

\displaystyle-\frac{(\gamma-1)c_{v}}{\bar{p}_{+}}\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}-1}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}[\bar{p}]\dot{p}_{-}+\frac{(\gamma-1)c_{v}}{\bar{p}_{+}}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{-}-\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}-\frac{[\bar{p}]}{\bar{q}_{-}}\Big)\sigma{q}_{en}(r)

\displaystyle-\frac{(\gamma-1)c_{v}}{\bar{p}_{+}}\Big(\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{p}_{-}}{(\gamma-1)c_{v}\bar{\rho}_{-}}+\frac{[\bar{p}]}{\gamma c_{v}}\Big)\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}+\Big(\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{-}-\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}-\frac{[\bar{p}]}{\bar{q}_{-}}\Big)\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{-}\Big)

(6.22)

\displaystyle\quad\times\int_{0}^{\dot{z}_{*}}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}z,

with

(6.23)

\displaystyle\kappa_{1}(r):=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}+\frac{\gamma-1}{\gamma\bar{p}_{+}}\Big)[\bar{p}],

(6.24)

\displaystyle\kappa_{2}(r):=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\bar{q}_{+}\bar{q}_{-}}-\frac{\gamma-1}{\bar{c}_{+}^{2}}\Big)(\bar{q}_{+}-\bar{q}_{-})-\frac{\kappa_{1}(r)}{\bar{q}_{-}},

\displaystyle\kappa_{3}(r):=

\displaystyle\Big(1-\kappa_{1}(r)-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{p}_{-}}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{p}_{+}}\Big)\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}}{\gamma c_{v}}

(6.25)

\displaystyle+\Big(\kappa_{1}(r)+\big(\frac{1}{\bar{q}_{+}}-\frac{(\gamma-1)\bar{q}_{-}}{\bar{c}_{+}^{2}}\big)\big(\bar{q}_{-}-\bar{q}_{+}\big)\Big)\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{q}_{-}}{\bar{q}_{-}}.

Notice that
\dot{g}_{1}=\dot{p}_{+}(\dot{z}_{*},r)
given by (
6.19
) involves with
\dot{p}_{-}
and
\dot{\theta}_{-}
, so we need to solve the initial-boundary value problem (
6.1
)-(
6.8
) at the same time.
Similarly as done in Section 4, we solve the initial-boundary value problem (
6.1
)-(
6.8
) as follows.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume that
(w_{en},q_{en})\in C^{7}([0,r_{0}])^{2}
, (
2.38
)-(
2.43
) hold, and the equations (
6.1
)-(
6.5
) with the boundary conditions (
6.6
)-(
6.8
) satisfy the compatibility conditions up to the seventh order, then there exists a sufficiently small positive constant
\dot{\sigma}_{L}
depending on
\overline{U}_{-}
and
L
, such that for any
0<\sigma<\dot{\sigma}_{L}
, there exists a unique solution
\dot{U}_{-}
to problem (
6.1
)-(
6.8
), which satisfies

(6.26)

\displaystyle\|\dot{U}_{-}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C_{(\overline{U}_{-})}\sigma\big(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}\big),

where the positive constant
C_{(\overline{U}_{-})}
only depends on
\overline{U}_{-}
and
L
. Moreover,

(6.27)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{j}(\dot{p}_{-},\dot{q}_{-},\dot{s}_{-})(z,0)=0,\quad j=1,3,

(6.28)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{w}_{-}(z,0)=0,

(6.29)

\displaystyle\dot{w}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0,\quad\partial_{r}(\dot{p}_{-},\dot{q}_{-},\dot{s}_{-})(z,r_{0})=0.

Proof.

This proof is divided into two steps.

Step 1:
In this step, we prove the unique existence of the solution
\dot{U}_{-}
that satisfies (
6.26
).
Applying (
6.3
), (
6.5
) and (
6.6
), direct calculations yield that

(6.30)

\displaystyle\dot{w}_{-}(z,r)=

\displaystyle\sigma w_{en}(r)-\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\int_{0}^{z}\dot{\theta}_{-}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau,

(6.31)

\displaystyle\dot{s}_{-}(z,r)=

\displaystyle-\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}\int_{0}^{z}\dot{\theta}_{-}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Substituting (
6.30
) and (
6.31
) into (
6.2
), further calculations yield that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{\theta}_{-}\Big)+\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{p}_{-}\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{-}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\Big)\int_{0}^{z}\dot{\theta}_{-}(\tau,r)\mathrm{d}\tau

(6.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\sigma w_{en}(r).

In addition, (
6.1
) can be rewritten as the following form:

(6.33)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\frac{1-\bar{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{-}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{p}_{-}\Big)-\partial_{r}\Big(r\bar{p}_{-}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{\theta}_{-}\Big)=0.

So there exists a potential function
\widehat{\Psi}_{-}
with
\widehat{\Psi}_{-}(0,0)=0
such that

(6.34)

\displaystyle\nabla\widehat{\Psi}_{-}=(\partial_{z}\widehat{\Psi}_{-},\partial_{r}\widehat{\Psi}_{-})=

\displaystyle\Big(r\bar{p}_{-}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{\theta}_{-},\,\,\frac{1-\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{-}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{p}_{-}\Big).

That is

(6.35)

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}_{-}=\frac{\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\partial_{z}\widehat{\Psi}_{-},\quad\dot{p}_{-}=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}\frac{\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}}{r}\partial_{r}\widehat{\Psi}_{-}.

By (
6.6
)-(
6.8
) and (
6.35
), we have

(6.36)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{-}(0,r)=0,\quad\partial_{z}\widehat{\Psi}_{-}(0,r)=0,\quad\widehat{\Psi}_{-}(z,0)=0,\quad\widehat{\Psi}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0.

Let

\displaystyle\Psi_{-}=\frac{\widehat{\Psi}_{-}}{r^{2}}.

Then (
6.35
) implies that

(6.37)

\displaystyle\dot{\theta}_{-}=r\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}{\Psi}_{-},\quad\dot{p}_{-}=\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}(2\Psi_{-}+r\partial_{r}{\Psi}_{-}),

and (
6.36
) yields that

(6.38)

\displaystyle{\Psi}_{-}(0,r)=0,\quad\partial_{z}{\Psi}_{-}(0,r)=0,\quad\partial_{r}{\Psi}_{-}(z,0)=0,\quad{\Psi}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0.

Substituting (
6.37
) into (
6.32
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}{\Psi}_{-}\Big)+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\Big(\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}{1-\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}(2\Psi_{-}+r\partial_{r}{\Psi}_{-})\Big)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{-}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\Big){\Psi}_{-}

(6.39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r^{2}}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\sigma w_{en}(r).

Let
\mathcal{D}_{-}:=\{(z,\mathbf{r}):z\in(0,L),\mathbf{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{4},|\mathbf{r}|<r_{0}\}
,
where
\mathbf{r}=(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})
and
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{4}r_{i}^{2}=r^{2}
. Let
\psi_{-}(z,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=\Psi_{-}(z,r)
, then by boundary condition (
6.38
) on
\{r=0\}
, it follows from Lemma
4.1
that
\psi_{-}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})
if and only if
\Psi_{-}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathcal{D}_{-})
.
Moreover, (
6.39
) becomes the following equation in
\mathcal{D}_{-}

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\Big(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\psi_{-}\Big)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\partial_{r_{i}}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}{1-\bar{M}_{-}^{2}}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\partial_{r_{i}}\psi_{-}\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big\{\frac{2}{r}\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}{1-\bar{M}_{-}^{2}}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\Big)-\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}}{\gamma c_{v}}\bar{w}_{-}-2\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\Big)\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\Big\}\psi_{-}

(6.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r^{2}}\bar{p}_{-}^{-\frac{1}{\gamma}}\sigma w_{en}(r),

with the following boundary conditions

(6.41)

\displaystyle{\psi}_{-}(0,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=0,\quad\partial_{z}{\psi}_{-}(0,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=0,

(6.42)

\displaystyle{\psi}_{-}(z,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},r_{4})=0,\quad\text{for}\quad|\mathbf{r}|=r_{0}.

Following the proof of Theorem
2.9
in Section 4, we can obtain the unique existence of the solution
\dot{U}_{-}
that satisfies (
6.26
), if the assumptions stated in the lemma hold.

Step 2:
In this step, we prove the conditions (
6.27
)-(
6.29
) hold.
First, by the equation (
6.3
) and the condition
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0
, we have

(6.43)

\displaystyle\dot{w}_{-}(z,r_{0})=\dot{w}_{-}(0,r_{0})=\sigma w_{en}(r_{0})=0.

By (
6.2
),
\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
,
\dot{w}_{-}(z,0)=0
and
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0
, we can obtain

(6.44)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\dot{p}_{-}(z,0)=0.

Similarly, it follows from (
6.3
),
\bar{w}_{-}(r_{0})=0
,
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0
, and (
6.43
) that

(6.45)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\dot{p}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0.

Take derivative on the both sides of the equation (
6.1
) with respect to
r
,

\displaystyle\frac{1-\bar{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\partial_{zr}\dot{p}_{-}+\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{1-\bar{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\Big)\partial_{z}\dot{p}_{-}-\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}-\frac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}+\frac{1}{r^{2}}\dot{\theta}_{-}

(6.46)

\displaystyle-\frac{\bar{M}_{-}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}-\partial_{r}\Big(\frac{\bar{M}_{-}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\Big)\cdot\dot{\theta}_{-}=0.

Applying (
3.28
), (
3.29
), (
6.44
) and
\lim\limits_{r\rightarrow 0}\frac{r\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)-\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)}{r^{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)
, then (
6.46
) implies that

(6.47)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0.

Taking derivative with respect to
r
on the both sides of the equation (
6.5
), we have

(6.48)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}(\partial_{r}\dot{s}_{-})+\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}\cdot\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}+\dot{\theta}_{-}\cdot\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{s}_{-}=0.

Applying
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0
and
\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}(0)=0
, we have

\partial_{z}(\partial_{r}\dot{s}_{-})(z,0)=0
.
Thus

(6.49)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\dot{s}_{-}(z,0)=\partial_{r}\dot{s}_{-}(0,0)=0.

Similarly, applying
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0
and
\partial_{r}\bar{s}_{-}(r_{0})=0
, we have

(6.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\dot{s}_{-}(z,r_{0})=\partial_{r}\dot{s}_{-}(0,r_{0})=0.

By similar calculations as done for (
6.49
) and (
6.50
), we have

(6.51)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}\dot{q}_{-}(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}\dot{q}_{-}(z,r_{0})=0.

Next, we will prove

(6.52)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{w}_{-}(z,0)=0.

(
6.3
) yields that

(6.53)

\displaystyle\partial_{z}\dot{w}_{-}+\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\dot{\theta}_{-}=0.

Taking twice derivatives with respect to
r
on the both sides of the equation (
6.53
), we have

(6.54)

\displaystyle\partial_{zrr}\dot{w}_{-}+2\big(\partial_{rr}\bar{w}_{-}+\partial_{r}(\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r})\big)\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}+\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\dot{\theta}_{-}+\big(\partial_{r}\bar{w}_{-}+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\big)\partial_{rr}\dot{\theta}_{-}=0.

Then it follows from
\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
,
\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
,
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0
,
\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0
, and (
6.54
) that

(6.55)

\displaystyle\partial_{zrr}\dot{w}_{-}(z,0)=0.

So by
\partial_{r}^{2}w_{en}(0,0)=0
, (
6.52
) holds.

Now we will prove that
\partial_{r}^{3}\dot{p}_{-}(z,0)=0
.
Taking twice derivatives with respect to
r
on the both sides of (
6.2
), we have

(6.56)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}(\partial_{z}\big(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-})\big)+\partial_{r}^{3}\dot{p}_{-}-\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-}\big)-\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\dot{w}_{-}\big)+\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{\gamma c_{v}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r}\dot{s}_{-}\big)=0.

Note that

(6.57)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}(\partial_{z}\big(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-})\big)\big|_{r=0}=\Big((\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2})_{rr}\partial_{z}\dot{\theta}_{-}+2(\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2})_{r}\partial_{zr}\dot{\theta}_{-}+\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\partial_{zrr}\dot{\theta}_{-}\Big)\big|_{r=0}=0.

So it follows from (
3.41
), (
6.44
), and Lemma
3.1
that

(6.58)

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{r}\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-}\big)\big|_{r=0}\\[5.69054pt]
=&\Big(\partial_{r}^{2}(\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r})\cdot(\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-})+2\partial_{r}(\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r})\cdot\partial_{r}(\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-})+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r}\partial_{r}^{2}(\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}}\dot{p}_{-})\Big)\big|_{r=0}\\[5.69054pt]
=&0.\end{array}

Similarly, we can show

(6.59)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\frac{1}{\gamma c_{v}}\frac{\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}^{2}}{r}\dot{s}_{-}\big)\big|_{r=0}=0.

Moreover, it follows from
\dot{w}_{-}(z,0)=0
,
\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
,
\partial_{r}^{2}\bar{w}_{-}(0)=0
, and
\partial_{r}\bar{\rho}_{-}(0)=0
, that

(6.60)

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\partial_{r}^{2}\big(\frac{2\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\dot{w}_{-}\big)\big|_{r=0}\\[5.69054pt]
=&\Big(\partial_{r}^{2}(\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r})\cdot(\bar{\rho}_{-}\dot{w}_{-})+2\partial_{r}(\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r})\partial_{r}(\bar{\rho}_{-}\dot{w}_{-})+\frac{\bar{w}_{-}}{r}\partial_{r}^{2}(\bar{\rho}_{-}\dot{w}_{-})\Big)\big|_{r=0}\\[5.69054pt]
=&0.\end{array}

Thus, (
6.56
)-(
6.60
) yield that

(6.61)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{3}\dot{p}_{-}(z,0)=0.

Finally and similarly, by tanking the third derivatives with respect to
r
on the both sides of the equations (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), and applying
\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,0)=0
, (
6.57
)-(
6.61
) and
\partial_{r}^{j}(\bar{p}_{-},\bar{q}_{-},\bar{\rho}_{-},\bar{s}_{-})(0)=0
,
j=1,3
, it is easy to obtain

(6.62)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{3}(\dot{q}_{-},\dot{s}_{-})(z,0)=0.

It completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Lemma
2.11
.

Proof of Lemma
2.11
.

Direct calculations yield that

\displaystyle{I}_{1}^{\prime}(z)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(r)(1-\kappa_{1}(r))\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}(r)-1}{\bar{\rho}_{-}(r)\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\partial_{z}\dot{p}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r

(6.63)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{r_{0}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(r)\kappa_{3}(r)\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r.

By employing (
6.1
), (
6.7
) and (
6.8
), it follows from (
6.63
) that

\displaystyle{I}_{1}^{\prime}(z)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(r)(\kappa_{1}(r)-1)\Big(\partial_{r}\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)+\frac{1}{r}\big(1+\frac{\overline{M}_{-}^{2}(r)\bar{w}_{-}^{2}(r)}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\big)\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\Big)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{r_{0}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}(r)\kappa_{3}(r)\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r

(6.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}i_{1}(r)\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r.

By applying the equation (
6.2
), it follows from (
6.64
) that

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\prime\prime}(z)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\partial_{z}\big(i_{1}(r)\dot{\theta}_{-}(z,r)\big)\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{i_{1}(r)}{\bar{\rho}_{-}(r)\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\partial_{r}\dot{p}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r+\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{i_{1}(r)\overline{M}_{-}^{2}(r)}{\bar{\rho}_{-}(r)\bar{q}_{-}^{4}(r)}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}(r)}{r}\dot{p}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r

(6.65)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{2i_{1}(r)}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}(r)}{r}\dot{w}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r-\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{i_{1}(r)}{\gamma c_{v}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}^{2}(r)}{r}\dot{s}_{-}(z,r)\mathrm{d}r.

It is easy to see that
{I}_{1}(0)={I}_{1}^{\prime}(0)=0
and

(6.66)

\displaystyle{I}_{1}^{\prime\prime}(0)=\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{2i_{1}(r)}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}(r)}{r}\dot{w}_{-}(0,r)\mathrm{d}r=2\sigma\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{i_{1}(r)}{\bar{q}_{-}^{2}(r)}\frac{\bar{w}_{-}(r)}{r}w_{en}(r)\mathrm{d}r=2\sigma I_{3}.

So it follows from (
6.64
) that there exists a
\hat{z}\in(0,z)
such that

(6.67)

\displaystyle{I}_{1}^{\prime}(z)=

\displaystyle 2\sigma I_{3}z+\frac{1}{2}z^{2}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}i_{1}(r)\partial_{z}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(\hat{z},r)\mathrm{d}r.

By applying (
6.26
), we have

\displaystyle{I}_{1}^{\prime}(z)\geq 2\sigma I_{3}z-\frac{C_{(\overline{U}_{-})}\sigma(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})})}{2}z^{2}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}|i_{1}(r)|\mathrm{d}r.

By (
2.48
), we have
{I}_{1}^{\prime}(z)>0.
Thus

\displaystyle\min\limits_{z\in(0,L_{*})}I_{1}(z)=I_{1}(0)=0,

\displaystyle\max\limits_{z\in(0,L_{*})}I_{1}(z)=I_{1}(L_{*})=\int_{0}^{L_{*}}\Big(2\sigma I_{3}z+\frac{1}{2}z^{2}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}i_{1}(r)\partial_{z}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(\hat{z},r)\mathrm{d}r\Big)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\qquad=L_{*}^{2}\Big(\sigma I_{3}+\frac{L_{*}}{6}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}i_{1}(r)\partial_{z}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-}(\hat{z},r)\mathrm{d}r\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq L_{*}^{2}\Big(\sigma I_{3}-\frac{C_{(\overline{U}_{-})}\sigma(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})})}{6}L_{*}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}|i_{1}(r)|\mathrm{d}r\Big).

Applying (
2.49
), it is easy to see that there exists a unique
\dot{z}_{*}\in(0,L_{*})
such that (
2.46
) holds.
∎

Finally, we will construct the approximate solution in the quasi-subsonic domain, that the iteration space
\mathcal{N}
below is based on. Applying (
6.28
)-(
6.29
), (
3.28
)-(
3.29
), (
6.9
)-(
6.10
) and (
6.18
)-(
6.22
), it is easy to check that (
5.50
)-(
5.51
) and (
5.173
)-(
5.178
) hold.
Then it follows from Theorem
5.3
, Theorem
5.7
and Lemma
2.11
that there exists a unique solution
\dot{U}_{+}
in the domain
\dot{\Omega}_{+}=\{(z,r)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\dot{z}_{*}<z<L,\,0<r<r_{0}\}
, which satisfies

\displaystyle\|(\dot{p}_{+},\dot{\theta}_{+},r\dot{w}_{+},\dot{q}_{+},\dot{s}_{+})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\dot{w}_{+}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}

(6.68)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\dot{C}_{+}\sigma\Big(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}+\|p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\Gamma_{ex})}\Big),

where the constant
\dot{C}_{+}
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
L
,
r_{0}
,
\dot{z}_{*}
and
\alpha
, and
\dot{z}_{*}
is given by Lemma
2.11
. Moreover

(6.69)

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{+}(z,0)=0,\quad\partial_{r}(\dot{p}_{+},\dot{q}_{+},\dot{s}_{+})(z,0)=0,

(6.70)

\displaystyle\dot{w}_{+}(z,r_{0})=0,\quad\partial_{r}(\dot{p}_{+},\dot{q}_{+},\dot{s}_{+})(z,r_{0})=0,

(6.71)

\displaystyle\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}(0)=\dot{\varphi}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)=\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}(r_{0})=0.

6.2.
Nonlinear iteration scheme

In this subsection, we will design a nonlinear iteration scheme to determine the shock solution to the nonlinear boundary value problem (
5.13
)-(
5.28
). Before doing that, an important property of the supersonic solution ahead of the shock front will be stated by the following lemma, which will be used to update the shock position.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
U_{-}:=\overline{U}_{-}+\delta U_{-}
, then

(6.72)

\displaystyle\|\delta U_{-}-\dot{U}_{-}\|_{C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})}\leq C_{L}\sigma^{2},

where the positive constant
C_{L}
only depends on
\overline{U}_{-}
and
L
.

Proof.

By applying the equations (
5.1
)-(
5.5
), (
6.1
)-(
6.5
), Theorem
2.9
and Lemma
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\delta U_{-}-\dot{U}_{-}\|_{C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|\partial_{z}\delta U_{-}\|_{C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})}\cdot\|\delta U_{-}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})}+\|\partial_{r}\delta U_{-}\|_{C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})}\cdot\|\delta U_{-}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})}+\|\delta U_{-}\|_{C^{3}(\overline{\Omega})}^{2}\Big)

(6.73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{L}\sigma^{2}(\|w_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})}+\|q_{en}\|_{H^{7}(\Gamma_{en})})^{2},

where the positive constant
C_{L}
only depends on
\overline{U}_{-}
and
L
.
∎

Now we can introduce the iteration scheme. Given a state
U=\overline{U}_{+}+\delta U
and the slope of the shock front
\varphi^{\prime}=\delta\varphi^{\prime}
, based on (
5.52
) in Theorem
5.3
, we know the shock position
\varphi(r_{0})={z}_{*}

must satisfy the following identity

(6.74)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\sigma p_{ex}(r)-g_{1}({z}_{*},r)\Big)\mathrm{d}r=\int\int_{\dot{\Omega}_{+}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(\delta U,\varphi)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r.

Take
K:=\dot{z}_{*}
in the transformation (
5.11
), then denote the domain
\Omega_{+}^{\dot{z}_{*}}
by
\dot{\Omega}_{+}
.
Define

\mathcal{S}(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime}):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\delta\theta(z,0)=0,\,\delta w(z,0)=0,\,\partial_{r}(\delta p,\delta q,\delta s)(z,0)=0,\,\partial_{r}^{2}\delta\theta(z,0)=0,\\
&\delta\theta(z,r_{0})=0,\,\delta w(z,r_{0})=0,\,\partial_{r}(\delta p,\delta q,\delta s)(z,r_{0})=0,\\
&\delta\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\delta\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)=\delta\varphi^{\prime}(r_{0})=0\end{aligned}\right\}

and

\mathscr{N}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime},{z}_{*}):\,(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime}),\,\mbox{\eqref{Rsolvability} holds},\,|\dot{z}_{*}-{z}_{*}|<C\sigma,\\
&\|(r\delta w,\delta q,\delta s)\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta w\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\varphi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}<C\sigma,\\
&\|(\delta p-\dot{p}_{+},\delta\theta-\dot{\theta}_{+})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta w-\dot{w}_{+}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\varphi^{\prime}-\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\\
&+\|(r\delta w-r\dot{w}_{+},\delta q-\dot{q}_{+},\delta s-\dot{s}_{+})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\end{aligned}\right\}

where the positive constant
C
depends only on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
p_{en}
,
w_{en}
,
p_{ex}
,
L
,
r_{0}
,
\dot{z}_{*}
and
\alpha
, and will be given later.

For
(\delta U;\delta\varphi^{\prime},{z}_{*})\in\mathscr{N}
with
\dot{\Omega}_{+}=\Omega_{+}^{{z}_{*}}
,
define the iteration mapping

(6.75)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}:(\delta U;\delta\varphi^{\prime},{z}_{*})\mapsto(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\varphi^{*}(r_{0})),

where
(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime})
is solved by problem (
5.13
)-(
5.28
) with (
5.41
)-(
5.42
), and
\vec{\mathcal{F}}:=(f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4},f_{5})
and
\vec{\mathcal{G}}:=(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},g_{4},g_{5})
are functions of
(\delta U;\delta\varphi^{\prime})
and
\varphi(r_{0})={z}_{*}
. Then
\varphi^{*}(r_{0})
is solved by (
6.74
) with
(\delta U;\delta\varphi^{\prime},z_{*})
being replaced by
(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\varphi^{*}(r_{0}))
.
The mapping can be rewritten by an operator
\mathcal{L}
as

(6.76)

\displaystyle(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime};\varphi^{*}(r_{0}))=\mathcal{L}(H(z,r);\vec{\mathcal{F}}(\delta U,\varphi);\vec{\mathcal{G}}(\delta U,\delta U_{-},\varphi);p_{ex}).

Obviously, (
6.68
)-(
6.71
) yield that
(\dot{U}_{+},\dot{\varphi}^{\prime},\dot{z}_{*})\in\mathscr{N}
, so the function space
\mathscr{N}
is non-empty.
We will prove that the mapping
\mathcal{T}
maps
\mathscr{N}
into
\mathscr{N}
, and then the mapping is contractive, to finish the proof of Theorem
2.13
by the Banach fixed point theorem.

First, we will prove that the iteration mapping
\mathcal{T}
is well defined.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists a positive constant
\sigma_{1}
with
0<\sigma_{1}\ll 1
, such that for any
0<\sigma\leq\sigma_{1}
, if
(\delta U;\delta\varphi^{\prime},{z}_{*})\in\mathscr{N}
, then
(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\varphi^{*}(r_{0}))\in\mathscr{N}
.

Proof.

We divide the proof into two steps.

Step 1
. In the first step, we will solve
(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime})
.
It follows from
(\delta{U};\delta{\varphi}^{\prime},{z}_{*})\in\mathscr{N}
that

(6.77)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=3}^{5}\|f_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=1}^{5}\|g_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}<C\sigma,

(6.78)

\displaystyle H(z,0)=H(z,r_{0})=0,\quad\partial_{r}f_{1}(z,0)=0,\quad f_{2}(z,0)=f_{2}(z,r_{0})=0,

(6.79)

\displaystyle f_{3}(z,0)=f_{3}(z,r_{0})=0.

So by Theorem
5.3
and Theorem
5.7
, there exists a unique solution
(\delta U^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime})
to the linearized problem (

) and

(6.80)

\displaystyle(\delta{U}^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{S}(\delta U^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime}).

Moreover, it follows from Theorem
5.3
and Theorem

again that

\displaystyle\|(\delta p^{*},\delta\theta^{*},\delta q^{*},\delta s^{*})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|r\delta w^{*}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta w^{*}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{2}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=3}^{5}\|f_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=1}^{5}\|g_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+\sigma\|p_{ex}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\Gamma_{ex})}\Big)

(6.81)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle C\sigma.

Moreover, direct calculations yield that

\displaystyle\|\delta p^{*}-\dot{p}_{+}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\theta^{*}-\dot{\theta}_{+}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

(6.82)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|f_{1}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|f_{2}-\dot{f}_{2}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\|g_{j}-\dot{g}_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\Big)

\displaystyle\|r\delta w^{*}-r\dot{w}_{+}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta q^{*}-\dot{q}_{+}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta s^{*}-\dot{s}_{+}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\sum_{j=3}^{5}\|f_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\|g_{j}-\dot{g}_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+\|\delta\theta^{*}-\dot{\theta}_{+}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\big(\|\partial_{r}(rw^{*})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\partial_{r}q^{*}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\partial_{r}s^{*}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\big)\Big)

(6.83)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle C\Big(\sum_{j=3}^{5}\|f_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\|g_{j}-\dot{g}_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+\|\delta\theta^{*}-\dot{\theta}_{+}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\Big)+C\sigma^{2}

and

(6.84)

\displaystyle\|\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime}-\dot{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\leq\|g_{5}-\dot{g}_{5}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}

where the positive constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
w_{en}
,
q_{en}
,
L
and
\alpha
.

By (
5.19
), it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\|f_{1}(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime},\delta z_{*})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\big(\|\partial_{z}\delta p\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\partial_{r}\delta p\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\big)\|\delta U\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\partial_{r}\delta\theta\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\|(\delta p,\delta q,\delta s)\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|(\partial_{z}\delta p,\partial_{z}\delta\theta)\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\|\delta\varphi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+|\varphi(r_{0})-\dot{z}_{*}|\|\partial_{z}\delta p\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\Big)

(6.85)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle C\sigma^{2}.

For
j=3,4,5
, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{j}(\delta U,\delta\varphi^{\prime},\delta z_{*})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Omega}_{+})}\leq C\big\|H(z,r)-\delta\theta\big\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{-\delta z_{*}+\int_{r}^{r_{0}}\delta\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau-(z-L)\delta\varphi^{\prime}\tan\delta\theta}{L-\dot{z}_{*}+(z-L)\delta\varphi^{\prime}\tan\delta\theta}\tan\delta\theta+(\tan\delta\theta-\delta\theta)\Big\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle C\Big(\big(|\delta z_{*}|+\|\delta\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\big)\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\big(\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\big)^{2}\Big)

(6.86)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle C\sigma^{2}.

Next, let us consider the estimates of

,
(j=1,\cdots,4)
. By (

\displaystyle g_{i}-\dot{g}_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{4}a_{ij}(G_{j}^{\sharp}-\dot{G}_{j})\qquad\text{for}\quad i=1,\cdots,4.

Thus, it suffices to estimate

\displaystyle+\alpha_{j}^{-}\cdot\Big(\delta U_{-}\big(\varphi(r_{0})-\int_{r}^{r_{0}}\delta\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,r\big)-\dot{U}_{-}(\varphi(r_{0}),r)\Big)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\delta U,\delta U_{-})\int_{0}^{1}D^{2}G_{j}(\overline{U}_{+}+t\delta U;\overline{U}_{-}+t\delta U_{-})\mathrm{d}t(\delta U,\delta U_{-})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{j}^{-}\cdot\Big\{\Big(\delta U_{-}\big(\varphi(r_{0})-\int_{r}^{r_{0}}\delta\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,r\big)-\dot{U}_{-}\big(\varphi(r_{0})-\int_{r}^{r_{0}}\delta\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,r\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Big(\dot{U}_{-}(\varphi(r_{0})-\int_{r}^{r_{0}}\delta\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,r)-\dot{U}_{-}(\varphi(r_{0}),r)\Big)\Big\}

(6.88)

\displaystyle+\alpha_{j}^{-}\cdot\big(\dot{U}_{-}(\varphi(r_{0}),r)-{\dot{U}}_{-}(\dot{z}_{*},r)\big).

)-(

)-(

), (

) and the fact that
(\delta U;\delta\varphi^{\prime},z_{*})\in\mathscr{N}
that for

\displaystyle\|g_{j}-\dot{g}_{j}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|\partial_{z}\dot{U}_{-}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\big(|\varphi(r_{0})-\dot{z}_{*}|+\|\delta\varphi^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\big)+\|\delta U_{-}-\dot{U}_{-}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\Big)

,

and

Then let us consider the estimate of

\displaystyle-\frac{(\rho_{-}q_{-}^{2}\cos\theta_{-}\sin\theta_{-}-\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\dot{\theta}_{-})(\varphi(r_{0}),r)+\bar{\rho}_{-}\bar{q}_{-}^{2}\big(\dot{\theta}_{-}(\varphi(r_{0}),r)-\dot{\theta}_{-}(\dot{z}_{*},r)\big)}{[\bar{p}]}.

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\|(\delta p,\delta q,\delta s)\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\theta\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}^{2}+\|\delta\theta_{-}-\dot{\theta}_{-}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\Omega})}\Big)

,

and

Step 2

,

Next, for

and

(6.109)

\displaystyle\int\int_{\dot{\Omega}_{+}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}\partial_{z}\dot{p}_{+}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r=\int\int_{\dot{\Omega}_{+}}\partial_{r}\big(r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\dot{\theta}_{+}\big)\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r=0,

where (
6.109
) comes from (
5.13
) with
f_{1}=0
and boundary conditions (
5.24
) and (
5.25
). Here we drop
\penalty\ {}^{*}
in (
6.108
) for the notational simplicity. By (
6.108
) and (
6.109
) with estimates (
6.80
)-(
6.81
) and (
6.102
), we have that for sufficiently small
\delta z_{*}
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\int\int_{\dot{\Omega}_{+}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}f_{1}(\delta U^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\delta z_{*})\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r\Big\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\big(\|\partial_{z}\delta p^{*}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\partial_{r}\delta p^{*}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\big)\|\delta U^{*}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\partial_{r}\delta\theta^{*}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|\delta\theta^{*}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\|(\delta p^{*},\delta q^{*},\delta s^{*})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\theta^{*}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\Big(\|(\partial_{z}\delta p^{*},\partial_{z}\delta\theta^{*})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\|\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+\|\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\|\partial_{z}\dot{p}_{+}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}\Big)

\displaystyle+C\|\partial_{z}(\delta p^{*}-\dot{p}_{+})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}|\delta z_{*}|

(6.110)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle C\big(\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot|\delta z_{*}|+\sigma^{2}\big),

where the positive constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
L
and
r_{0}
.

Applying Lemmas
2.11
and
6.2
and estimtes (
6.105
)-(
6.110
), (
6.104
) yields that

\displaystyle{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*};\delta{U}^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*};\delta{U}^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})-{I}^{*}(0;\delta\dot{U}_{+},\dot{\varphi}^{\prime},\dot{U}_{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{1-\overline{M}_{+}^{2}}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\Big(\dot{g}_{1}(\dot{z}_{*},r)-\dot{g}_{1}(\dot{z}_{*}+\delta z_{*},r)\Big)\mathrm{d}r+O(1)\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\delta z_{*}+O(1)\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{r_{0}}\frac{\overline{M}_{+}^{2}-1}{\bar{\rho}_{+}\bar{q}_{+}^{2}}r\bar{p}_{+}^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\partial_{z}\dot{p}_{+}(\dot{z}_{*},r)\mathrm{d}r\delta z_{*}+O(1)\sigma(\delta z_{*})^{2}+O(1)\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\delta z_{*}+O(1)\sigma^{2}

(6.111)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}^{\prime}(\dot{z}_{*})\delta z_{*}+O(1)\sigma(\delta z_{*})^{2}+O(1)\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\delta z_{*}+O(1)\sigma^{2},

where
O(1)
are bounded functions in
C^{1,\alpha}
, which only depend on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
L
and
r_{0}
.
Then (
6.111
) implies that, as long as
\sigma
is small enough, we have

(6.112)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{I}^{*}}{\partial{\delta z_{*}}}(0;0,0,\dot{U}_{-})=I_{1}^{\prime}(\dot{z}_{*})+O(1)\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}\neq 0.

By applying the implicit function theorem, for sufficiently small
\sigma>0
, there exists a solution
\delta z_{*}
satisfying
{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*};\delta{U}^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})=0
, that is,
\delta z_{*}
satisfies equation (
6.74
), and estimate

(6.113)

\displaystyle|\delta z_{*}|\leq\Big|\frac{O(1)\sigma^{2}}{I_{1}^{\prime}(z_{*})}\Big|\leq C\sigma,

where constant
C
only depends on
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
w_{en}
,
q_{en}
,
L
,
r_{0}
and
\gamma
. So (
6.103
) follows.

Therefore, (
6.80
)-(
6.81
) and (
6.102
)-(
6.103
) yield that
(\delta{U}^{*};\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\varphi^{*}(r_{0}))\in\mathscr{N}
.
∎

Finally, Theorem
2.13
will be proven if we show that the mapping
\mathcal{T}
is contractive in
\mathscr{N}
, which will be done in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.4
.

There exists a positive constant
\sigma_{3}
with
0<\sigma_{3}\ll 1
, such that for any
0<\sigma\leq\sigma_{3}
, the mapping
\mathcal{T}
defined in (
6.75
) is contractive.

Proof.

Suppose that
(\delta U_{k},\delta\varphi_{k}^{{}^{\prime}},z_{k})\in\mathscr{N}
for
k=1,2
. By Lemma
6.3
, there exist
(\delta{U}_{k}^{*},\delta\varphi_{k}^{*^{\prime}},z_{*k})\in\mathscr{N}
with estimate (
6.103
).
Finally, by a similar argument as done in Lemma
6.3
to establish estimate (
6.102
) via replacing
(\delta U^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime},\varphi^{*}(r_{0}))
and
(\delta\dot{U}^{+},\delta\dot{\varphi}^{{}^{\prime}},\dot{z})
by
(\delta{U}_{k}^{*},\delta\varphi_{k}^{*^{\prime}},z_{*k})
for
k=1,2
, we can prove for sufficiently small
\sigma
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|(\delta p_{2}^{*}-\delta p_{1}^{*},\delta\theta_{2}^{*}-\delta\theta_{1}^{*})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|(\delta q_{2}^{*}-\delta q_{1}^{*},\delta s_{2}^{*}-\delta s_{1}^{*},r\delta w_{2}^{*}-r\delta w_{1}^{*})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle+\|\delta w_{2}^{*}-\delta w_{1}^{*}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\varphi_{2}^{*^{\prime}}-\delta\varphi_{1}^{*^{\prime}}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sigma\Big(\|(\delta p_{2}-\delta p_{1},\delta\theta_{2}-\delta\theta_{1})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|(\delta q_{2}-\delta q_{1},\delta s_{2}-\delta s_{1},r\delta w_{2}-r\delta w_{1})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

(6.114)

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\delta w_{2}-\delta w_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta{\varphi_{2}}^{\prime}-\delta{\varphi_{1}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+|z_{1}-z_{2}|\Big).

Because the proof is long and similar, we omit the details for the shortness. To conclude the proof of this lemma, we need to estimate
|z_{*2}-z_{*1}|
. Let
\delta z_{*k}=z_{*k}-\dot{z}
. Notice that

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*2},\delta{U}^{*}_{2},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})-{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*1},\delta{U}^{*}_{1},\delta{\varphi_{1}^{*}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*2},\delta U^{*}_{2},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})-{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*1},\delta U^{*}_{2},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})

\displaystyle+{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*1},\delta U^{*}_{2},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})-{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*1},\delta U^{*}_{1},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{1}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\partial{I}^{*}}{\partial(\delta z_{*})}(\delta z_{*t},\delta U^{*}_{2},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})\mathrm{d}t(\delta z_{*2}-\delta z_{*1})

(6.115)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{(\delta U^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime})}{I}^{*}(\delta z_{*1},\delta U^{*}_{t},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{t}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})\mathrm{d}t\cdot(\delta U^{*}_{2}-\delta U^{*}_{1},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime}-\delta{\varphi^{*}_{1}}^{\prime}),

where

\displaystyle\delta\xi_{*t}:=t\delta z_{*2}+(1-t)\delta z_{*1}\quad\delta U^{*}_{t}:=t\delta U^{*}_{2}+(1-t)\delta U^{*}_{1}\quad\delta{\varphi^{*}_{t}}^{\prime}:=t\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime}+(1-t)\delta{\varphi^{*}_{1}}^{\prime}.

Similarly as done in step 2 of the proof of Lemma
6.3
, we have

(6.116)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial{I}^{*}}{\partial(\delta z_{*})}(\delta z_{*t},\delta U^{*}_{2},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})=\frac{\partial{I}^{*}}{\partial(\delta z_{*})}(0,0,0,\dot{U}_{-})+O(1)\sigma^{\frac{3}{2}}+O(1)\sigma^{2}.

Moreover, it is easy to see that

(6.117)

\displaystyle\nabla_{(\delta U^{*},\delta{\varphi^{*}}^{\prime})}I^{*}(\delta z_{*1},\delta U^{*}_{t},\delta{\varphi^{*}_{t}}^{\prime},\delta U_{-})=O(1)\sigma,

where
O(1)
only depends on
{I}^{\prime}(\dot{z}_{*})
,
\dot{z}_{*}
,
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
L
,
p_{ex}
,
w_{en}
,
q_{en}
and
\alpha
.
Therefore, (
6.115
)-(
6.117
) yield that

\displaystyle|\delta z_{*2}-\delta z_{*1}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Big(\|(\delta p^{*}_{2}-\delta p^{*}_{1},\delta\theta^{*}_{2}-\delta\theta^{*}_{1})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|(\delta q^{*}_{2}-\delta q^{*}_{1},\delta s^{*}_{2}-\delta s^{*}_{1},r\delta w^{*}_{2}-r\delta w^{*}_{1})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

(6.118)

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\delta w^{*}_{2}-\delta w^{*}_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta{\varphi^{*}_{2}}^{\prime}-\delta{\varphi^{*}_{1}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}\Big),

where the constant
C
only depends on
\dot{z}_{*}
,
\overline{U}_{\pm}
,
L
,
p_{ex}
,
w_{en}
,
q_{en}
and
\alpha
.
Therefore, it follows from (
6.114
) and (
6.118
) that there exists a positive constant
\sigma_{3}
with
0<\sigma_{3}\ll 1
, such that for any
0<\sigma\leq\sigma_{3}
,

\displaystyle\|(\delta p_{2}^{*}-\delta p_{1}^{*},\delta\theta_{2}^{*}-\delta\theta_{1}^{*})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|(\delta q_{2}^{*}-\delta q_{1}^{*},\delta s_{2}^{*}-\delta s_{1}^{*},r\delta w_{2}^{*}-r\delta w_{1}^{*})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle+\|\delta w_{2}^{*}-\delta w_{1}^{*}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta\varphi_{2}^{*^{\prime}}-\delta\varphi_{1}^{*^{\prime}}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+|z_{*2}-z_{*1}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(\|(\delta p_{2}-\delta p_{1},\delta\theta_{2}-\delta\theta_{1})\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|(\delta q_{2}-\delta q_{1},\delta s_{2}-\delta s_{1},r\delta w_{2}-r\delta w_{1})\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\|\delta w_{2}-\delta w_{1}\|_{C^{0,\alpha}(\overline{\dot{\Omega}_{+}})}+\|\delta{\varphi_{2}}^{\prime}-\delta{\varphi_{1}}^{\prime}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(\dot{\Gamma}_{s})}+|z_{1}-z_{2}|\Big).

So the mapping
\mathcal{T}
defined in (
6.75
) is contractive.
∎
